Preferred, so nothing stops and the secret is kept: make the new key, then the board's owner or an administrator re-saves each webhook with their new key (`PUT /api/v1/webhooks/{id}` with `{"active": false}`, then `{"active": true}`), which binds it to that key, and only then revoke the old key. Otherwise, revoke the old key first, and the board's owner or an administrator switches each webhook on with their key, which gives it a new secret for its receiver (the code review's CR6).
- **Webhooks made before this release** record no key. Each keeps sending while the account that set it up holds a working key made before the release, and is switched off once it has none. At the first start that runs the schedulers each board's owners get one mail ("A webhook on your board will stop when its key ends") listing them with the earliest date each will stop (the latest end among that account's keys from then; later if that account keeps or replaces such a key, the code review's CS2), or "no date" where one of those keys has neither an expiry nor a deadline; the system log has one `WARN` under `Webhooks` for each. To keep one, re-save it with a key: `PUT /api/v1/webhooks/{id}` with `{"active": false}`, then `{"active": true}`, by the board's owner or an administrator, which binds it to that key. Where the schedulers are off nothing is mailed and the next start that runs them says it again; with no mail set up one more `WARN` says the owners were not told. See [Deployment](deployment.md#the-release-that-switches-off-a-webhook-whose-api-key-ends-card-2893).

Whose account you can change, set a password for, send an invite to, reset the two-step sign-in of (card 2773) or delete depends on your role and theirs, and is the same for all five. An invite is on the list because its link sets the account's password:

| Their account is | An administrator can manage it | The superadmin can manage it |
|------------------|--------------------------------|------------------------------|
| An ordinary user's | ✅ | ✅ |
| Another administrator's | ❌ | ✅ |
| The superadmin's | ❌ | Only their own, and not to delete it |

Each row shows only the controls you may use on that account, by the same rule the server refuses by (one copy of it, in `src/lib/account-rule.ts`, which the page and the routes both ask):

| The row is | What it shows |
|------------|---------------|
| An account you may manage | The role select, 🔑, 📧, 🛡️ and 🗑️ (📧 dimmed for an account that uses two-step sign-in) |
| Your own | 🔑 alone, which changes your own password and asks for the one you have. Your role, an invite and deleting yourself are not offered, and the server refuses each there: a change to your own role is answered 403 whatever it is, and nothing sent with it is saved. The superadmin can change an administrator's role |
| Another administrator's, to an administrator | 🔒 and nothing else |
| The superadmin's, to anyone else | 🔒 and nothing else |

An administrator who tries to reset another administrator's password, send them an invite, change their role or delete them by a request made another way is refused, and a page that sends one shows the reason. The role select, the invite and the delete used to be shown on rows where they could only be refused, the administrator's own row among them, where one choice in the role select made them an ordinary user.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    Key["🔑 on a row of Admin → User Management"] --> Whose{"Whose row?"}
    Whose -->|"your own"| Own["The dialog asks for your current password<br/>and a new one, as the Account page does.<br/>Your other sessions are signed out.<br/>Your API keys and webhooks keep working"]
    Whose -->|"an ordinary user's"| Set["The password is set, that user is signed<br/>out everywhere, their API keys and<br/>unused invites stop working, the webhooks<br/>they set up are switched off and their<br/>report schedules paused"]
    Whose -->|"another administrator's"| Super{"Are you the superadmin?"}
    Super -->|"yes"| Set
    Super -->|"no"| Refused["Refused: only the superadmin<br/>can reset an administrator's password"]
```

Your own password is never reset from this page without the one you have. On your own row the 🔑 dialog asks for your current password, as the password form on the **Account** page does. It keeps your API keys and the webhooks you set up, and says so: "Your API keys and the webhooks you set up keep working. To end the keys and switch off those webhooks too, change your password on the Account page." It pauses your report schedules, as every password change does since card 2894, and says that too ("Your report schedules are paused."); the message after gives how many ("Password changed: 2 report schedules paused"). To end the keys and webhooks with the change, change your password on the Account page and tick the box there. Your own row has no 📧, and the route refuses one: an invite link sets a password and asks for none, so nobody sends one to themselves. Nor has it 🛡️ (card 2773): the reset asks for no second factor, and would give whoever held your session and password a link to set up their own. Your own email is not changed through user management either, because an invite goes to it: it is changed on the Account page, which asks for your password. So no user-management route sets your own password, or moves the address a new one would be sent to, without asking for the password you have. The superadmin's row shows 🔒 to everyone else, and 🔑 to the superadmin, which is the same own-password dialog.

The same rule holds for the rest of the Admin page. **Restoring a backup** replaces every account with the backup's, passwords and roles included, and **Reset Everything** clears every account, so both are the superadmin's alone, with the export (the backup destinations and the mail settings are every administrator's since card 2395): see [Who May Do What](#who-may-do-what). Any administrator could once run either, and so could make themselves anybody.

### Resetting Two-Step Sign-In

Someone who has lost the phone their authenticator app is on, and has no recovery code left, asks an administrator to reset their two-step sign-in (card 2773). **Someone with a recovery code left needs no administrator** since card 2772: see [Helping someone replace a phone](#helping-someone-replace-or-lose-a-phone) below. 🛡️ is also how you give a set-up link to an account that has no authenticator and needs one: one past its 14 days after the release, one whose set-up was abandoned (card 2769), or one made with a password whose set-up link was lost (card 2851).

**🛡️ Reset two-step sign-in** is on every row that has 📧: the accounts you may manage, by the same rule as 🔑 and 📧 (an ordinary user's for an administrator; an administrator's or an ordinary user's for the superadmin), never your own and never the superadmin's. It asks first, and for your password and a code (one of the [forty](#your-password-asked-again)):

> Reset ann's two-step sign-in? Their authenticator and recovery codes stop working, they are signed out everywhere, their API keys end, the webhooks they set up are switched off, their report schedules are paused, and you are given a link, once, to pass on to them yourself. You will be asked for your password and a code from your authenticator app.

**Confirm** then, in one step: deletes their authenticator and recovery codes, sets their count of wrong codes back to nought, signs them out everywhere, ends any invite not yet used and any sign-in or set-up link they had, **ends every API key of theirs and switches off every webhook they set up** (card 2915, 2026-11-18), pauses every report schedule of theirs that is on (card 2894), and issues one new link with which they set up a new authenticator. Their password is kept: they lost a device, not their password. The keys end, always, with no box to keep them: you cannot tell a lost phone from a stolen one, and an API key skips the second factor, so a key made by whoever had the phone and the password would go on reading every board after the reset. A webhook goes with the keys, as on a password reset: switched off for good, and switched on again only by the board's owner or an administrator, which gives it a new secret. A schedule is paused because it sends a board's activity whether or not anyone is signed in, so one made by whoever took the phone would outlive the reset; they turn back on the ones they recognise on the **Reports** page once their new authenticator is set up, and make new keys for the scripts they recognise. The confirm gives no counts (yours could be older than a key made since); the dialog after gives them. An account with no authenticator can be reset too, which gives it a new link (one that expired, say, or an account a restore left without one). The button reads "…" while it is on its way, and 📧 and 🛡️ on every row stay greyed until it is answered and the dialog below is closed. The link opens in the dialog an invite's link uses, with its own words:

> **🛡️ Two-step sign-in was reset**
>
> ann's two-step sign-in was reset. Give them the link yourself: it is shown once, and works for seven days. Their 2 report schedules were paused. Their 2 API keys (made 2026-11-01 and 2026-11-10) were ended and the 1 webhook they set up (made 2026-11-12) switched off; the lists are in the notice mailed to them. ann was sent a notice that holds no link.
>
> Give ann this link yourself, by a way only they read: it is shown once, here. It works for seven days, and stops working if their two-step sign-in is reset again. Whoever opens it sets up the authenticator of ann's account, and the account still needs its password. Do not open it yourself.
>
> `https://kanban.example.com/enrol/…` **📋 Copy**
>
> It is not shown again. If it is lost, reset ann's two-step sign-in again with 🛡️, which issues a new link and ends this one.

- **The link is never mailed.** Whoever has the phone may know the password and may have changed the account's email address, so a mailed link could go straight to them. You give it to the person yourself, by a way you know reaches them (in person, a phone call to a number you already had), and **Done** clears it from the page; it is in no toast, address or history. With the link alone nobody gets in: its page names the account and sends them to sign in with the account's password, which then leads to the set-up (card 2769). The link is used up only when the set-up is confirmed.
- **Pass on what the dialog's first line says** (card 2894; card 2915, 2026-11-18). It says how many of their report schedules were paused ("Their 2 report schedules were paused.", nothing when none was on), and how many working API keys were ended and webhooks switched off, with the day each was made ("Their 1 API key (made …) was ended, and they had no webhook they set up; …", "They had no API key and no webhook they set up." where there was neither). It names none of them (card 2894's code review): you may not be able to open the board a webhook is on, and this page never shows a key's name; the notice mailed to them names each, and the line says whether it went ("the list is in the notice mailed to them", or "the notice with the list was not sent: tell them how many ended and the day each was made"). Tell the person when you give them the link: they make new keys for the scripts they recognise once signed in, and ask the board's owner to switch back on any webhook they recognise. One they did not make means someone else had their password: have them change it. Where the link is not shown, the line gives the counts alone ("Their 1 API key was ended."), and nothing where none ended.
- **What settings they set** (card 2915). Where you, the superadmin, reset an administrator who set the mail settings, the App URL or a backup destination still in force, the line goes on "They set the mail settings, 1 backup destination and the App URL: review them on the admin page." It names no destination, and the reset removes none of them (making them a user, disabling or deleting them does): look each over under Email / SMTP, Application Settings and Backup. **Your message is the channel that reaches them for certain**: the notice below may not go, and their mailbox may be what was taken.
- **The account's address gets a notice instead**, where mail is set up: "Your DumpFire two-step sign-in was reset", naming you and when; saying their authenticator and recovery codes no longer work, or, for an account that had no authenticator, that its set-up was begun again with a new link; that they have been signed out everywhere; and that an administrator will give them the link themselves, never by email. Since card 2894 it also says how many of their report schedules were paused, where one was; since card 2915, where the reset ended a key or switched off a webhook, "The reset also ended your API keys and switched off the webhooks you set up:" with both lists, each with when it was made, then to make new keys for the scripts they recognise once their new authenticator is set up, that the board's owner or an administrator switches back on a webhook they recognise, which gives it a new secret, and that one they did not make means someone else had their password: change it, and tell an administrator. It ends "If you did not ask for this, tell an administrator straight away. Use only a link an administrator hands you directly." It holds no link. The dialog's first line ends "ann was sent a notice that holds no link." when it went; where mail is set up and it did not go, it adds "The notice to ann was not sent: …" with the reason, and the reset stands. Where mail is not set up, nothing is sent and nothing more is said.
- **No link shown.** If, while the notice was out, you were signed out or made an ordinary user, the account became one you may no longer manage, or another reset replaced the link, no dialog opens: a warning that stays until closed says "ann's two-step sign-in was reset, but its link cannot be shown to you now. Run the reset again to issue a new one." Press 🛡️ again once you can.
- **Refused, with nothing changed**: "Manage your own two-step sign-in on the Account page" for your own account, "Only the server's host can reset the superadmin's two-step sign-in, with scripts/mfa-reset.mjs" for the superadmin's, "Only superadmin can reset an admin's two-step sign-in", and, when the server has no address to make the link from, the invite's words ("This server has no address configured to make the link from…"): set the App URL under Application Settings.
- **The trail.** One `CRITICAL` line under `AUTH` on the Audit page's Live Console, under your name: `Two-step sign-in reset for user 12 ("ann") by "chief": removed 1 factor(s) and 10 recovery code(s), ended 2 session(s), 0 pending sign-in(s), 0 invite(s) and 2 API key(s), switched off 1 webhook(s) they set up (id 7), and paused 2 report schedule(s) (ids 3, 5); an enrolment link was issued`. It never holds the link. A link withheld adds a `WARN` line saying why.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    Press["🛡️ on ann's row"] --> Ask["Confirm, with your password"]
    Ask --> Refused{"Refused?"}
    Refused -->|"yes: your own, the superadmin's,<br/>an admin's (not superadmin), no App URL"| Nothing["Said; nothing changed"]
    Refused -->|"no"| Reset["Authenticator and codes deleted,<br/>signed out, invites and links ended,<br/>API keys ended, webhooks switched off,<br/>report schedules paused, one new link"]
    Reset --> Notice["Mail set up: a notice<br/>to ann's address, no link"]
    Notice --> Still{"Still yours to give?"}
    Still -->|"yes"| Dialog["The dialog: the link, once.<br/>Give it to ann yourself"]
    Still -->|"no"| Warn["A warning: run the reset again"]
```

**📧 is refused for an account that uses two-step sign-in.** An invite sets the account's password and leads on to setting up an authenticator, which such an account already has, so 📧 is dimmed on its row and its title says "This account uses two-step sign-in. Reset it first, then send the invite"; the server refuses one made another way the same. Reset it with 🛡️ first: once it has no authenticator, 📧 is offered again.

**When the password is lost too, set the password first, then reset two-step sign-in.** Setting a password ends every set-up link the account has, so a password set after 🛡️ ends the link you just gave. With 🔑: 🔑, then 🛡️. **An invite is still the better way to give a password back** where mail works, since nobody but its holder then knows the password, and an invite leads straight on to setting up a new authenticator, whose confirm signs them in and remembers their browser (see [During an attack](#modifying-users) above). 📧 is refused while the account has an authenticator, so: 🛡️, then 📧, and they set their password and a new authenticator with the invite alone; the 🛡️ link is ended by the invite's use and is not needed. Changing the account's email address or role also ends its link.

**The superadmin's own authenticator** is reset only on the server's host, with a command the host runs: `docker exec <container> node scripts/mfa-reset.mjs <username>`. It does what 🛡️ does, for any account by its username, and prints the link on the host's terminal; it also works when no administrator can sign in. Since card 2915 it ends the account's API keys and switches off its webhooks too, unless the host adds `--keep-api-keys` (`node scripts/mfa-reset.mjs --keep-api-keys <username>`): the superadmin often holds the keys an integration uses, and whoever runs it on the host may know the phone was only lost. `--` before the username names one that starts with "-". See [If the Superadmin Loses Their Authenticator](deployment.md#if-the-superadmin-loses-their-authenticator).

**After a restore**, reset the two-step sign-in of each account the restore names as having no second factor, and give each its link: see [Restoring from Backup](#restoring-from-backup). A restore also takes away every account's leave to set up on its password alone, but the leave of an account still on this server. **After the key is changed** on the host ([Changing the Key](deployment.md#changing-the-key)) nobody needs a reset: every authenticator is moved to the new key at that start, and keeps working. An authenticator that start names as unreadable needs its account reset.

### Two-Step Sign-In: What People See

Since card 2769 a right password no longer signs anyone in. Everyone, administrators and the superadmin included, proves a code from an authenticator app on their phone, or a recovery code, every time they sign in. There is no switch to turn it off. How it works is in [Two-Step Sign-In](authentication-security.md#two-step-sign-in-card-2769).

- **Signing in.** After the password, a page headed "Two-step sign-in" names the account and asks for the 6-digit code from the app, typed as `123456` or `123 456`. "Use a recovery code instead" swaps the field for a recovery code; while codes are locked the page opens on the recovery code. The page lasts ten minutes; after that, or after five wrong codes on it, they sign in again from the start. It works without JavaScript too.
- **Setting up.** An account with no authenticator is shown a QR code to scan, with the key written out in groups of four for typing in by hand, then asked for the code the app shows. Then it is shown ten recovery codes, each good once, which are not shown again, and **I have saved these codes** finishes it and signs them in.
- **Wrong codes.** Five wrong codes in a row pause code entry for 15 minutes, ten for an hour, fifteen for four hours, twenty and twenty-five for a day, and thirty lock it. A browser the person has signed in with before counts its wrong codes apart from every other browser, so someone guessing elsewhere does not pause them there. **A recovery code always works**, paused or locked, and clears the pause and the lock. With several authenticators (card 2772) a code from any of them works, and each wrong code counts once for each authenticator that can be checked on this server, so codes are no easier to guess: with two, the third wrong code in a row pauses; with three, the second.
- **After a right code**, if wrong codes were entered for the account since its last sign-in, or someone gave the right password and then a wrong code or left the sign-in unfinished, a page says so, with the last sign-in's time and address and the account's API keys and webhooks, and asks them to change their password if it was not them. **Continue** loads the home page. The page is shown once for what happened: the count of wrong codes then starts again from nought, and a warning raised before this sign-in is not shown again at the next. The warning itself stays on the account, at the top of the Account page (card 2772), until the person clears it there or changes their password (below).
- **If the server has lost its MFA key**, someone with an authenticator is told their authenticator cannot be checked right now, and a recovery code still signs them in; someone who would set one up is told it cannot be set up right now.
- **Mail**, where mail is set up and its password entered: a notice when two-step sign-in is set up (listing the account's API keys and webhooks), when a recovery code is used, when code entry is paused or locked, at every tenth wrong recovery code in a row, at most once an hour when someone gets the password right and the code wrong or leaves the sign-in unfinished, and (card 2772) when an authenticator is added or removed or a new set of recovery codes is made. None holds a code or a link. See [Security Notices](notifications-email.md#two-step-sign-in-notices-card-2769).
- **The Account page's 🛡️ Two-step sign-in** (card 2772), under Change Password: the last sign-in and any wrong codes since, the person's authenticators (each with its name, when it was added and when last used), **Add an authenticator** (a name, then the password and a code, then a QR code to scan and the new phone's code; at most three), **Remove** (greyed on the only one, "Add the new one first, then remove this one."; removing one signs their other browsers out), the recovery codes left (a warning at three or fewer) and **Make new recovery codes** (ten, shown once). Each change mails the owner.
- **The warning at the top of the Account page** (card 2772), "⚠️ Was this you?", after someone gave the right password and then a wrong code, or left a sign-in unfinished: **Change my password** takes them to that form, and **That was me**, with the password and a code, clears it. It is cleared only as shown: if a newer warning arrived meanwhile, they are told "A newer warning arrived. Read it, then dismiss it again." Changing their own password clears it too. Your password reset of their account does not.

#### Helping someone replace or lose a phone

Since card 2772 a person replaces a phone themselves, on the Account page, while they can still sign in:

- **A new phone, the old one still at hand**: **Add an authenticator** with the new phone, then **Remove** the old one. The section never removes the last one, so add first.
- **A lost phone, a recovery code left**: sign in with a recovery code, add the new phone, remove the lost one, then **Make new recovery codes** and change the password. The section says so: "Lost a phone? Sign in with a recovery code, add your new phone here, remove the lost one, then make new recovery codes and change your password." Removing it signs out every other browser of the account, which ends a session the lost phone may still hold.
- **A lost phone, no recovery code left**: 🛡️ (above).
- **At most three authenticators**: "You have three: remove one first." Each wrong code counts once for each authenticator that can be checked on this server, so with three, one wrong answer at a confirmation signs that browser out; the dialog says so before it is sent (its line counts every authenticator held, so where one cannot be opened here it says more than is counted, never less).
- **The removal is refused** with "Your other authenticator cannot be checked on this server right now, so this one cannot be removed. Ask an administrator." when no other authenticator of theirs can be opened on this server (the MFA key missing or changed): see [If the Key Is Lost](deployment.md#if-the-key-is-lost) on the host, or reset with 🛡️.

**Helping someone who cannot sign in.**

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    Q["Someone cannot sign in"] --> W{"What does the page say?"}
    W -->|"code entry is paused"| P["Wait, or use a recovery code:<br/>it signs in and clears the pause"]
    W -->|"codes are locked"| R["Use a recovery code.<br/>None left: 🛡️ reset"]
    W -->|"phone lost, a recovery code left"| Self["Sign in with it, then on the Account page:<br/>add the new phone, remove the lost one,<br/>new recovery codes, new password"]
    W -->|"phone lost, no recovery code"| S["🛡️ reset, give them the link.<br/>The superadmin: the host's command"]
    W -->|"needs a set-up link"| T["🛡️ gives one.<br/>Or 📧 an invite"]
    W -->|"could not email you a set-up link"| U["Put the mail right under Email / SMTP<br/>and the App URL, or give a 🛡️ link"]
    W -->|"set-up left after an invite"| V["📧 the invite again, or 🛡️"]
    W -->|"the superadmin left the first-run set-up"| X["On the host:<br/>node scripts/mfa-reset.mjs name"]
```

- **A paused or locked account** waits, or uses one of its recovery codes. The lock lifts only with a recovery code or a reset. Someone with no recovery code left, or no phone, needs 🛡️.
- **Set-up abandoned after an invite.** The invite was spent when the password was set. Send the invite again with 📧, or reset with 🛡️ and give them the link.
- **The superadmin's first-run set-up abandoned.** The superadmin has a password and no authenticator, and nobody manages that account on this page. On the host: `docker exec <container> node scripts/mfa-reset.mjs <username>`, which prints a link to give them, and ends any API key the account made (add `--keep-api-keys` to keep them, card 2915) (see [If the Superadmin Loses Their Authenticator](deployment.md#if-the-superadmin-loses-their-authenticator)).

### The Release That Turns On Two-Step Sign-In

The update that brings card 2769 signs everybody out once and forgets every browser that has signed in before. Nobody has an authenticator yet, so everybody sets one up at their next sign-in. It also ends every invite still waiting to be used (card 2849, which keeps an invite's link only as a fingerprint from then on): those links stop working, and you send again the ones still wanted. The host's steps and checks are in [Deployment](deployment.md#the-release-that-turns-on-two-step-sign-in-card-2769).

**Before it:**

1. **Every running instance is stopped** by whoever runs the host. An older copy still running would sign people in on a password alone.
2. **Check the App URL** under Application Settings, where mail is set up: it must be this server's real address, not empty and not `http://localhost:3000`. The release mails each person a set-up link made from it.
3. **Review the API keys, and revoke any nobody recognises.** No page lists another account's keys: the host's read-only query lists them all ([Deployment](deployment.md#the-release-that-turns-on-two-step-sign-in-card-2769), check W3). Each holder revokes their own on the Account page; 🔑 on a row ends every key of that account. Keys keep working through the release, so a key made by someone who had a password is how they would stay in.
4. **Consider resetting every administrator's two-step sign-in**, and any account whose password may be known: 🛡️ for an administrator (it is the superadmin's to do), and the host's command for the superadmin. A reset account needs the link you give it, and can never be set up on its password alone.
5. **Keep the list of invites still waiting.** The host lists them, with the account each was for, before the update ([Deployment](deployment.md#the-release-that-turns-on-two-step-sign-in-card-2769), check W15). It is the only time they can be listed: the update ends them.

**After it:**

- **Sessions are shorter from the release** (card 2775). An administrator is signed out after 12 hours without using DumpFire, and after 24 hours in any case; everyone else after 3 days unused and 7 days in any case. Signing in again means the password and the authenticator code again. Only a save or a full page load counts as use: a tab left open on its own, which only checks in the background, does not keep a session alive, and neither does a click on a link inside the app, which fetches data without loading a page. A session's code is kept in the database only as a fingerprint, so a copy of the database holds no working sign-in. Nothing is asked of you for this: everybody is already signed out once by the update.
- **Every invite sent before the update and not yet used no longer works.** Its link shows "This invite link no longer works…". Send again with 📧 each one on the list from step 5 that is still wanted; the new link works as before. Used invites, accounts and API keys are untouched.

- **Where no mail host is configured**, every account that existed at the release with no authenticator and no set-up link may set one up on its password alone, for 14 days. Whoever holds that password can do it first. After the 14 days it needs a link from you. The Users panel shows, under each such account not yet set up, "may set up on password until `<date>` UTC", and your own row shows yours. 🛡️ on one ends its leave at once and gives it a link instead. A set-up begun on the password is refused at its last step if the 14 days end, or a mail host is set up, meanwhile.
- **Clearing the mail settings within the 14 days is your decision to reopen this.** It turns every account still marked back to setting up on its password alone, until the 14 days end. Leave the mail set up, or reset the accounts you are unsure of.
- **Where a mail host is configured**, the first right password at such an account emails it a set-up link, good for seven days, and says so on the sign-in page; another sign-in within a day is told when it went. After a day a new link is mailed, and the older one stops working once the new mail has gone; a mail that fails leaves the older link working. While the owner is part-way through setting up from a mailed link, a sign-in elsewhere is told "Two-step sign-in is being set up for this account from the link we emailed…", and nothing more is mailed. The mail names when and from where the password was used, so it also tells the owner if it was not them. The panel shows no date while a mail host is set up: the password leads to the mail, not the set-up. If the mail cannot go (no password entered on this server, no App URL), the person is told "We could not email you a set-up link just now…": put the mail right, or give a 🛡️ link.
- **A working copy** made with Import Backup takes every account's leave to set up on its password: give its users 🛡️ links.
- **An account made after the release** has no such leave: create it with no password so it gets an invite, or give it a 🛡️ link.

### The Release That Gives Older API Keys a Deadline

The update that brings card 2772 gives every API key that exists on that day a deadline, 60 days later, the same date for every key: a key made before two-step sign-in was made on a password alone. A key with no expiry, or one later than the deadline, is marked; a key that expires sooner anyway, and every key made afterwards, is not. The host's steps, with the read-only queries, are in [Deployment](deployment.md#the-release-that-gives-older-api-keys-a-deadline-card-2772).

- **Before it, the key holders are told.** No page lists another account's keys: the host runs the read-only query, which lists each key with its holder's name and address, and you tell each holder the date and what to do. Scripts and integrations stop on that date if nobody keeps their key.
- **What a holder does.** On **My Account → API Keys** each such key reads "⚠️ Made before two-step sign-in: stops working on *date time* UTC unless you keep it", with **Keep this key**. Pressing it, with the password and a code, clears the deadline and leaves the key itself unchanged: nothing that uses it needs a change. Every key also shows its expiry now, "Expires *date time* UTC" or "No expiry".
- **On the date** a key not kept is answered `401` `Invalid or expired API key`, as an expired key is, and its row reads "Stopped working on *date time* UTC. Generate a new key." with no Keep. It cannot be revived: its holder generates a new key and puts it where the old one was used.
- **A fortnight before the date**, the host's second query lists the keys still not kept: remind their holders.
- **Reset Everything** empties the keys and the setting: nothing is left to keep, and new keys have no deadline.
- **Going back** to an older image after this update makes keys past their deadline work again while it runs: treat it as a security event.

### The Release That Asks for the Password Before Report Mail

The update that brings card 2751 puts the Reports page's mail behind your password and a code: making a report schedule, adding an address to one, switching one on, widening what it reports (its **Include** filter, from anything but **All work**), and **Email** on a report, for every address, your own and the company's among them. Anyone holding a signed-in browser could do each of these before without typing anything, and a schedule keeps sending after the browser is closed and after the password is changed. There is no migration and nothing to set. The host's steps are in [Deployment](deployment.md#the-release-that-asks-for-the-password-before-report-mail-card-2751).

- **Make sure mail is set up before the update.** At the first start after it, each person with a report schedule that is on is emailed once, "Your DumpFire report schedules", listing each of theirs that is on, what it reports on and every address it is sent to, and asked to delete any they do not recognise. With no mail set up nobody is emailed: the system log has one line for each schedule that is on (`WARN` under `REPORTS`, "Report schedule *n* "*name*" of "*owner*" … is on at this release, … sent to: …") and one `WARN` line naming the people not told. Read them on the Audit page and tell those people yourself. It is said once, once every email has gone: a later start says nothing (it is said again only after a restore of a backup made before the update). If an email does not go, a `WARN` line names who was not told, and the next start emails everyone again. A copy started with `SCHEDULERS=off`, such as a rehearsal, emails nobody.
- **A schedule somebody does not recognise.** Its owner deletes it on the **Reports** page (only they see it there), changes their password, and tells you. Since card 2894 (2026-11-18) changing their own password pauses every schedule of theirs that is on, as a 🔑 reset, an invite and a 🛡️ two-step reset do (see [What a reset switches off, and getting it back](#modifying-users)); they turn back on the ones they recognise. Removing one of their authenticators does not pause them, but its email lists the schedules that are on. Schedules that existed before the update keep running unless they are deleted or paused.
- **What people notice.** **Email** and **+ New Schedule** gain a **Your password** box and a code box; switching a schedule on opens **Turn on this schedule?**, naming every address it will email; switching one off asks nothing. A schedule made, given an address, switched on or widened sends its owner an email saying so, and the system log names every address it is sent to; a report emailed by hand is logged with the addresses it reached. **Reload every open Reports tab** after the update: an older page sends no password and is refused, "Enter your password and a code from your authenticator app to confirm this. Nothing was changed.", with nothing saved or sent.
- **API keys are not affected**: no key can reach these pages' routes.

### Disabling, Enabling and Deleting an Account

**Disable first** (card 2671, 2026-11-18). To take an account away, press ⛔ ("Disable user") on its row in **Admin → User Management**. The confirmation says what will happen and asks for your password and a code: "Disable user "bob"? They can no longer sign in. At once their sessions, invites, pending sign-ins, API keys and the links to the requests they made end, the webhooks they set up are switched off and their report schedules paused; none of this comes back if the account is enabled again. …" It names the teams the account is the only owner of (they keep it as owner until you make someone else one), for an administrator, what they set that goes with them: the mail settings, the App URL and the backup destinations; and, whatever the role, their address where it is one of the backup failure addresses, which the disable takes out (a restore that brings it back has it taken out again). The message afterwards counts what ended ("bob was disabled: ended 2 sessions, 1 API key and 1 request link, switched off 1 webhook and paused 1 report schedule."), names any settings removed, and says if the notice to the account could not be sent, which is so when the disable removed the mail settings.

A disabled account cannot sign in: the right password is answered as a wrong one. Nobody can assign it, share a board or a team with it, include it in a decision, link a person to it or send it a request, and the pickers leave it out; nothing is mailed to it but the notice that it was disabled. Its name stays wherever it is shown, on cards, comments and teams, and its row reads "Disabled *date* by *name*" and offers ✅ and 🗑️ in place of 🔑, 📧, 🛡️ and ⛔. A restore keeps it disabled ([Restoring from Backup](#restoring-from-backup)). You cannot disable your own account or the superadmin's, and only the superadmin can disable an administrator.

**Enable** (✅) gives back sign-in alone, with the account's own password and authenticator: "Enable user "bob" again? … Their API keys, webhooks, report schedules and request links stay ended: they make or turn on again the ones they need. If the account may have been used by someone else, reset its password and its two-step sign-in once it is enabled." An account the superadmin disabled is enabled, or deleted, by the superadmin alone: another administrator sees neither ✅ nor 🗑️ on it. A disabled account's password, invite and two-step sign-in cannot be reset until it is enabled. See [Disabled Accounts](authentication-security.md#disabled-accounts-card-2671).

**Delete** (🗑️) is offered only on a disabled account. It asks for confirmation, and your password (card 2701), and says what will happen. A disabled user can be deleted whatever they have done in DumpFire: uploaded files, saved templates, created milestones or recorded dependencies. An account that is not disabled is refused, "Disable the account first", and nothing is changed. Deleting also takes their comments, so disable and keep the account unless the person and their comments really must go.

| Deleted with the user | Kept |
|-----------------------|------|
| Their comments | Files they uploaded |
| Their API keys, already ended when the account was disabled | Milestones, templates and dependencies they created |
| Their report schedules. Those reports are no longer sent | Boards and cards they created |
| Their team memberships, board memberships and card assignments | Requests addressed to them |
| Their sessions, already ended when the account was disabled | Their XP, which stays on the dashboard's XP Leaderboard (on every board's header until 26 October 2026) |
| | Webhooks they set up, **switched off** (card 2168), and then naming nobody |
| The mail settings, the App URL and the backup destinations still where they set them (card 2397), removed already when an administrator's account was disabled: see [Who May Do What](#who-may-do-what) | Settings somebody else has moved since, settings moved back to where somebody else put them, and settings saved before migration 0059 |
| | The person linked to the account in the [people directory](#people-directory), **marked Left**, unlinked, and any personal decision link of theirs cancelled (card 2675) |

**The person goes to Left.** Deleting an account marks the person it was linked to as having left, with the date, so a person whose account was taken away on purpose cannot be sent a personal decision link to the same address by accident. The system log has a `PEOPLE` line for it, `Person 12 "Ann Lee" marked left: their account 7 was deleted; 0 decision links cleared (by admin)`, never the address. **Mark active** on their row brings them back, as a deliberate step of yours; a decision that had them with that account still asks you to clear it before they are sent a personal link.

Where settings went, the message after the delete stays until closed and names them ("User deleted. What ann had set was removed: …"), and the system log has a `WARN` line under `SMTP` or `BACKUP` for each kind.

The confirmation says what the disable already ended and what the delete still takes (card 2671): "Delete user "bob" for good? The account is disabled already, and its sessions, API keys and webhooks ended with that; deleting it also takes their comments, report schedules, team and board memberships and card assignments, and cannot be undone. …" The system log's line for the delete counts the webhooks it switched off and names them by id, for the board's owner to find, `… 1 webhook(s) they set up were switched off (id 4); …`: normally `0 webhook(s)`, and `0 API key(s)` beside it, since the disable switched them off and ended the keys already. A count above 0 is an account disabled by a restore or from the host with what it held still there, which the delete then ends.

After the delete:

- **Templates they created, and milestones of theirs that belong to no board, can be managed by administrators only.** A milestone that belongs to a board is still managed by whoever can edit that board.
- **A board they created stays, and its other members and teams keep their access.** The user was its owner: a board's creator is its owner. Unless another member is stored as an owner of the board, the board then has no owner, and only an administrator can change who it is shared with, delete it, or put it under a card. No screen can make someone an owner now (the sharing dialog offers Owner only on a row already stored as one); a member shared as Owner before 14 October 2026 keeps it.
- **Requests addressed to them stay in the system**, and only an administrator can then accept, reject or reply to them.
- **Requests they made stay, and their mail goes to the address the account had when it was deleted** (card 2171). While the account was there, a request made signed in was mailed at the account's email as it was at the time of each mail. The delete writes the account's email onto each of its requests first, in the same all-or-nothing step, so the team's replies and the progress mails go on reaching the person at their last address, as for a request made without an account. Requests of an account deleted before this change keep the address stored when they were made.
- **Activity entries they made keep their name**, without a link to an account.

The delete is all or nothing. If it fails, the user and everything of theirs is as it was, and the page says the delete failed.

**What is recorded, so that no restore undoes it** (card 2996, 2026-11-20). Every delete, disable, enable and role change, and the role every account is made with, is written to the server's **record of account endings**: the account's number and creation time, what happened and when, and for a delete a fingerprint of its password hash (never the hash, and not something a password can be checked against). Every password change is written there too (card 2997): your own on the Account page, 🔑, an invite used and Reset Everything each record the fingerprint of the password hash they replace. The record is kept by every import and by Reset Everything, as the system log is. So an import of an older backup brings a deleted account back **disabled and inert**, with no password or second factor that works; keeps a disabled account disabled; gives back the lower role an account has here; and never brings back a password replaced here. See [Restoring from Backup](#restoring-from-backup).

**An account the record names as deleted cannot be enabled.** Enable on such an account (one a restore brought back) is refused before your password is asked: "The record of account endings names this account as deleted, and a restore brought it back: it cannot be enabled. Delete it; then make a new account for anyone who returns." **Delete it before making the new account:** its username and email address would otherwise block the new one. Signing in as it, or using an API key of it, is refused even if a host edit enables it. A `deleted` entry in the record cannot be lifted: a new account is the way back.

**An account the record keeps disabled is the superadmin's to enable** (card 2996's code review). Where a restore or the start disabled an account by the record (a backup's entry, or an account this server had before it recorded endings), Enable by an administrator is refused before the password: "Only the superadmin can enable an account the record of account endings keeps disabled (a restore or the start brought its disable)". An account an administrator disabled on this server is still enabled by an administrator.

You cannot delete your own account or the superadmin. Only the superadmin can delete an administrator, or an account the superadmin disabled.

**An account that is the only owner of a team is not deleted** (card 2377, 2026-10-21). The delete takes the account out of every team, so each team it alone owned was left with no owner, run by administrators alone; a team keeps at least one owner ([Team Owners and Members](#team-owners-and-members)). The delete is refused, nothing is changed, and the toast names the teams, in name order: "This account is the only owner of: Alpha, Beta. Make someone else an owner or delete those teams first." ("… delete that team first" for one). Make another member of each an owner (**Admin → Team Management**, their row's picker, **Change**), or delete the team, then delete the account. A team the account owns with someone else, or is only a member of, does not stop the delete.

**The confirmation says so beforehand** (card 2380, 2026-10-21). For an account that is the only owner of one or more teams, as the admin page last read its teams, the confirmation's second sentence names them, in name order: "They are the only owner of: Alpha, Beta, so the delete will be refused: make someone else an owner or delete those teams first." ("… delete that team first." for one). Press **Cancel** and change the teams first. **Confirm** still sends the delete, and the server decides: if someone was made an owner since the page was loaded (in another tab, or by another administrator), the delete goes through; otherwise the toast above says it was refused. For an account that alone owns no team the confirmation is as before.

---

### Settings Held for Review (Card 2922)

When an account's access is ended with its API keys, the mail settings, the App URL and the
backup destinations it set are **held for the superadmin's review**: nothing is sent to them until
the superadmin keeps or removes each. That happens on:

- **Reset password** of an administrator (the dialog says what they set, which will be held);
- **🛡️ Reset two-step sign-in** (its confirm says so);
- **📧 Send invite**, when the invite is used, for backup destinations alone: where the account set
  the mail settings or the App URL in force, the invite is refused until its two-step sign-in is
  reset, which holds them, and the confirm says so before it is sent;
- the holder's own password change with "end my keys" ticked (the Account page's box says so, and
  is offered for the settings alone too);
- the host's `node scripts/mfa-reset.mjs <username>` unless `--keep-api-keys`.

A disable, a demotion to user and a delete still **remove** what the account set, as before.

**An import of a backup whose seal does not check out holds what it brings in that reaches outside** (card 3009), when it is restored as it is: the mail settings where they send through another place than this server's did before the import, the App URL where it differs from this server's (also where the server's own `ORIGIN` is the image's placeholder, `http://localhost:3000`), and each backup destination at a place this server held none at. Each is held as above, by nobody, because it was "restored from a backup whose seal did not verify": in such a file they are only its word. Keep or remove each here before sending any invite. A hold counts only where the import wrote one: one this server already held at that place (a setter's, carried over the backup) is not counted again (its code review, FP8). A backup this server sealed holds nothing, and a working copy of such a file removes them instead: the mail settings and destinations as every working copy does, and its App URL too, so links begin with this server's own address (FP3).

Such a hold is told in its own words wherever it is met (FP4): the list says the setting "came in with a backup whose seal did not verify" and "From a backup whose seal did not verify" where a setter's hold names who set it; a mail it stops says "the mail settings are held for the superadmin's review, as they came in with a backup whose seal did not verify, so the mail host is only the file's word"; a backup run, Test or Connect of such a destination says "it came in with a backup whose seal did not verify, so where it sends is only the file's word"; and each start's `[WARN] [STARTUP]` line says "(restored from a backup whose seal did not verify, so where they send is only the file's word)" in place of a setter. None of them names a setter, says "user unknown" or says anybody's access was ended.

**A restore holds what this server's record says it removed** (card 3004). A backup destination, the mail server or the App URL removed or moved here since the backup was made comes back held, "removed here", listed as "From a backup made before it was removed on this server"; from this server's own backup, one the record has never named and this server does not hold is held as "not in this server's record", listed as "From a backup, and not in this server's record of where it sends". One an ending held here, then cleared (by Reset Everything, say), comes back held as setter access ended, listed as "From a backup made before it was held on this server". None names a setter. A mail they stop says "the mail settings are held for the superadmin's review, as they came back with a restore though they were moved or removed on this server since the backup was made, or are not in this server's record of where it sends …"; a backup run, Test or Connect of such a destination "it came back with a restore, though it was removed on this server since the backup was made or is not in this server's record of where it sends"; and each start's `[WARN] [STARTUP]` line "(came back with a restore though removed on this server since the backup was made, or not in this server's record of where it sends)" or "(held as this server's record of where it sends says, set by no account named)". **Keep** records the place as wanted again, so the same backup restored later leaves it in force; **Remove** records it removed. Reset Everything keeps the record.

**Settings held for review**, at the top of the admin page, lists each held setting: where it sends
(never a password or secret), who set it and since when. Every administrator sees the list; while
anything is held the superadmin also sees a banner on every admin page, and each settings panel has
a one-line pointer to the list.

- **Keep** (the superadmin, password and code): the setting is used again exactly as shown. The
  confirm shows where it sends; if it changed since the list was read, Keep is refused and the list
  must be read again. Its setter is unchanged.
- **Remove** (the superadmin, password and code): the setting is removed. Its hold stays, so the
  same setting saved again, or brought back by a restore (a destination under any name at the same
  place), is held again. Removing held **mail** or a held **App URL** also ends every invite not
  yet used, every two-step set-up link, every request link and every decision participant's
  personal link on the server, as each was mailed through the one with a link beginning with the
  other: send fresh invites and links once mail is set up again.

Links already mailed through held mail settings stay usable until they are removed, so decide
quickly. While the mail settings are held, the mail panel's Save is refused ("keep or remove it
first"); so is the App URL's while it is held. A held backup destination cannot be renamed, saved as
another type or removed by a save, nor (card 2941) pointed elsewhere: a save that changes its host,
bucket, folder, folder path or OneDrive user or drive ID is refused ("where it sends its copies
cannot be changed by a save … keep or remove it first"). Sending an invite to an account whose own mail
settings or App URL are in force is refused: reset its two-step sign-in first.

While mail is held, nothing is mailed: invites, resets and notices are refused or say the mail is
held. While a destination is held, each backup run names it in Backup History as held, and Test,
Google Drive Connect, and a save that renames, retypes or removes it are refused. A two-step reset
whose only link address would be the held App URL is refused before anything changes: disable the
account or reset its password, set `APP_URL` on the host or save the App URL yourself, then reset.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    E["Reset, invite used,<br/>two-step reset, own change<br/>ending keys, host reset"] --> H["Held for review:<br/>nothing sent"]
    H -->|"Keep"| U["In use again"]
    H -->|"Remove"| T["Removed; held again<br/>if saved back"]
```

## Team Management

### Creating Teams

1. Go to the **Teams** page from the dashboard, or to **Admin → Team Management** (**+ Add Team**)
2. Click **Create Team**
3. Name the team and choose an emoji. You are made its owner
4. Add members. On the Teams page or **Admin → Team Management**, choose the person under **Add member…**, then their role, **Member** or **Owner**, beside **Add**; it starts at Member (card 2309 on the admin page; card 2355, 2026-10-21, on the Teams page, which added every member as Member). While an add is on its way a second press of **Add** sends nothing (card 2374, 2026-10-21: a double click sent it twice and said "Member added" twice); Add keeps the keyboard's focus. Someone added meanwhile elsewhere, and so already in the team, has their role set to the one chosen, and the page says "*name* was already in the team; role set to *Member* (or *Owner*)" rather than "Member added" (the card 2374 review)
5. Change a member's role: choose **Member** or **Owner** in the picker on their row, then press **Change** beside it. Choosing alone saves nothing (card 2376, 2026-10-21: the picker saved as it was chosen, so a keyboard user arrowing through it on Windows saved each role passed); **Change** is dimmed, and does nothing, until a role other than theirs is chosen. A role chosen and not saved is forgotten once they are removed from the team, or the page reads them back gone or already holding that role, so it never comes back on their row when they are added again (the card 2376 review). "Role changed" says it was saved; a change that is refused or gets no answer is said, and the picker shows the role they have again (card 2355, 2026-10-21; no page could change it). While a change is on its way the picker and Change stay where they are, with the keyboard's focus, and are marked busy, and a screen reader is told "Saving *name*'s role…"; another choice on the picker meanwhile goes back to the role being saved, and a second Change sends nothing (changes on two members' rows at once are each saved). If the person was taken out of the team meanwhile (by someone else, or on another page), the change puts them back in with the role chosen, and it says "*name* was no longer in the team and has been added again as *Member* (or *Owner*)" rather than "Role changed" On the Teams page the picker is on the rows an owner (or an administrator) may manage, and not on your own row, as ✕ is not; on the admin page it is on every row

### Team Owners and Members

A team's role says who may run the team; it gives no access to boards. The access a team gives is its share's role on each board (editor or viewer), the same for owners and members.

**Every change is recorded** (card 3002). A team made, a member added, a role changed, a member removed and a team deleted are each recorded by the identity of the team and the account, so a restore of an older backup never brings back a member removed here, or a role lowered here. A team deleted here and brought back by a restore comes back empty of the members and shares it had when it was deleted; delete it again on the Teams page if it is not wanted.

| | Owner | Member |
|---|:---:|:---:|
| Delete the team | ✅ | ❌ |
| Rename the team (through the API alone, `PUT /api/teams/{id}`: no page offers it) | ✅ | ❌ |
| Add and remove members, and change their role | ✅ | ❌ |
| Open the boards shared with the team | ✅ | ✅ |

**What an owner's adding gives.** An owner can add any account to the team, and each person added at once has the team's role, editor or viewer, on every board shared with the team, and the team's requests in their inbox. A board's owner has no say: the board was shared with the team, and whoever the team's owners add has it. Make someone a team's owner only if you would share every one of its boards with whoever they choose.

An administrator may do all of it on every team without being in it. Whoever creates a team is its owner, so a team made on the admin page is owned by the administrator who made it; to let someone else run it, add them as **Owner**, or change their role to Owner on their row. **A team keeps at least one owner** (card 2373, 2026-10-21). Making the last owner a member, or removing them, is refused for everyone, administrators included: "A team needs at least one owner. Make someone else an owner first, or delete the team." Make another member an owner first, then change or remove the first; or delete the team. There is no "leave team": a member asks an owner to remove them. Deleting an account takes it out of every team, so an account that is a team's only owner cannot be deleted until someone else is made an owner or the team is deleted (card 2377, 2026-10-21; see [Disabling, Enabling and Deleting an Account](#disabling-enabling-and-deleting-an-account)). A disabled account stays on its teams and is named there, and cannot be added to a team or made its owner until it is enabled (card 2671). A team left with no owner from before these rules (including by deleting its only owner's account before 21 October 2026) is run by administrators until one of them makes someone its owner again.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    Admin["Administrator"] -->|"any team"| Manage["Delete, rename (API),<br/>add and remove members"]
    Owner["Team owner"] -->|"their team"| Manage
    Member["Team member"] --> Boards["The boards shared<br/>with the team"]
    Owner --> Boards
```

### Sharing Boards with Teams

When you share a board with a team, all team members inherit the team's board role (editor or viewer). New members added to the team automatically gain access.

**Shares, members and a board's visibility are recorded** (card 3002). Sharing a board with a person or a team, changing the role, taking the share away and making a board public or private are each recorded in the same transaction, so a restore keeps a share removed or a board made private here as it is now. See [Restoring from Backup](#restoring-from-backup).

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    Board["📋 Project Board"] -->|"Shared with"| Team["👥 Dev Team"]
    Team --> Alice["👩‍💻 Alice\neditor"]
    Team --> Bob["🧑‍💻 Bob\neditor"]
    Team --> Charlie["🧑 Charlie\neditor"]

    Board -->|"Individual"| Diana["👩 Diana\nviewer"]
```

**Taking someone off a board leaves their assignments** (card 2180). A user removed from the
board, or from the team it is shared with, stays assigned to its cards, and is no longer
mailed about them: the "Card moved" and "Unblocked" mails go only to the assignees who can
open the board the card is on. The same goes for an assignee whose card is moved to a board
they cannot open. Current members are mailed as before. Unassign someone who should no
longer be listed on a card.

---

## People Directory

The people directory (card 2674, 2026-11-03) is the list of the people who can be consulted on or told of a decision, with an account or without one, and the groups they are in. Administrators keep it, on a page of its own: **Admin → 👥 People Directory → Open the people directory**, or `/admin/people`. Anyone signed in who is not an administrator is sent from the page to the dashboard, and the routes behind it refuse them with `403` `Forbidden`. Decisions read it: their participants are picked from it, and each participant's address and account are taken from it again when a decision opens (cards 2676 and 2697).

A person is a full name, an email address, a job title (which may be empty), the account they are linked to if they have one, their groups, and whether they are **Active** or have **Left**. A person needs no account: many of the people a decision consults never sign in. A group gives nothing: no board, no access, no mail. It is a name for a set of people, to pick them together.

**A person's account link is recorded** (card 3002). Linking a person to an account, changing the account and unlinking them (in **+ Add person**, **Edit** or **Add from accounts**) are recorded by the identity of the person and the account, so a restore of this server's own backup never links again a person unlinked here, and one whose seal does not check out links a person to an account only where this server already did.

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> Active: + Add person, or Add from accounts
    Active --> Left: Mark left, or their account deleted (date stamped, decision links cancelled)
    Active --> Left: a restore of an unsealed backup that did not hold them as they are (card 3020, recorded by DumpFire)
    Left --> Active: Mark active, with password and code (date cleared)
    Active --> Active: Edit; linking, unlinking or a new address cancels decision links
    Left --> Left: Edit; linking, unlinking or a new address cancels decision links
    Active --> [*]: Delete (cannot be undone)
    Left --> [*]: Delete (cannot be undone)
```

Every change is a line in the system log, context `PEOPLE`, level INFO, under the administrator who made it, readable on `/audit`: the person or group by id and name, and what changed, as `Person 12 "Ann Lee" changed: email, groups; 0 decision links cleared (by admin)`. A line never holds an address. A change that is refused writes no line, and a save that changes nothing writes none either.

### People

The table shows each person's name, email, job title, groups, account and status. It shows the **Active** people at first; **Left** and **All** above it change that, and the search box matches name, email, job title and group names.

- **+ Add person** opens a form: **Full name** (1 to 200 characters), **Email**, **Job title** (up to 200, may be left empty), **Account** (**None**, or an account no other person is linked to: see [Linking a Person to an Account](#linking-a-person-to-an-account)) and the groups to tick. **Edit** on a row opens the same form for that person. Ticking groups here replaces all of the person's groups; a group's own form does the same from the other side ([Groups](#groups)).
- **A change of a person's email or account asks for your password** (card 2697). A decision takes each participant's address and account from the directory as it opens, so the directory decides where their personal links go. When you change the **Email** or the **Account** in **Edit**, a **Current password** field appears and the save sends it; a wrong one keeps the form open with the field cleared. A new name, job title, groups and **Mark left** ask nothing. **Mark active** asks for your password and a code (card 3020): it lets the person be mailed about decisions again, at their address, so it opens a dialog naming the person and their address, as stored, before it sends. **+ Add person**, **Add from accounts** and **Delete** always ask (card 2697's code review, 2026-11-04): each gives a person an address and perhaps an account, or ends their links and frees their address, so the add form and the Add from accounts dialog show the **Current password** field from the start, and Delete's confirmation asks for it.
- **The address is unique across the directory.** It is stored in lower case, and no two people may have the same one in any case, people who have left included. A second is refused with `409` "Email already in the directory: Ann Lee". Where the person who has it is linked to an account, the message says which, "Email already in the directory: Ann Lee (linked to account ann)", so you can tell an entry that came from an account from one typed in. Correct or delete the entry that has it first.
- **Mark left** records a leaver. The row says "Left" with the date and moves from the Active list to Left. Their account link and their groups are kept, and **Mark active**, with your password and a code, brings them back as they were. Mark left is the way to record that someone has gone; it also cancels their personal decision links ([below](#linking-a-person-to-an-account)).
- **A person marked left by a restore** (card 3020) is one a backup whose seal did not verify brought in with an address or account this server did not hold for them, or with a status other than active or left. The people change record holds a row for each, by DumpFire, source `restore`, with the status and date before and after and no address. The page shows each person's status as stored (a status other than active or left, from such a file, shows as it is, never as "Active"), and, for a person who is not active, their address with the code points of any character that is not plain ASCII and their name in printable form; **Edit** shows "Address on file: …" the same way. Check the address before **Mark active**: its confirmation shows the address their mail goes to again, and it is tied to that address, so where the address has changed since the page was read it is refused with `409` "This person's address has changed since the page was read: read it again and check the address before you mark them active. Nothing was changed."
- **Delete** removes a person from the directory for good. It is for an entry made by mistake, a duplicate, or someone who must not be listed any more. It asks first, in the app's confirm dialog, with your password, and cannot be undone. Their group memberships go with them, their personal decision links stop working, and their address may be used for a new person afterwards. The message after it says how many decision links were cancelled, where there were any.

**What Delete does not erase.** The confirmation says so, and you should know it before you rely on Delete to remove someone:

- **The system log** keeps every `PEOPLE` line that names them, the delete's own among them (`Person 12 "Ann Lee" deleted; 0 decision links cleared (by admin)`).
- **Decision records** keep what they recorded of them: name, address, job title and groups as they were when the person was included, with only the tie to the directory entry emptied. A record is meant to show who was told what, and Delete does not rewrite it.
- **Backups** taken before the delete hold the person, and restoring one brings them back.

### Linking a Person to an Account

A person is linked to an account in their form, under **Account**. It offers **None**, the person's own account, and every account no other person is linked to, each with its username and address; one person per account, and the two addresses need not match. An account linked to someone else meanwhile is refused with `409` "That account is already linked to Ann Lee". Choosing **None** unlinks them. A disabled account is not offered, and is refused with `400` "userId 7 is a disabled account" (card 2671): it could be neither mailed nor answer; a person already linked to one stays linked, and is not mailed about a decision until the account is enabled.

**An account's address is not verified.** Whoever holds an account sets its address themselves, on My Account, and DumpFire does not confirm it. So an address that matches proves nothing: anyone can set theirs to a colleague's. Before you link an account to a person, check that it is that person's account. The form says so under the field: "An account's address is set by its holder and is not confirmed: check it is this person before linking." This matters more once decisions exist, because a person's account is then how they open and answer the decisions they are on.

**Linking or unlinking a person's account, a new address, or marking them left, cancels their personal decision links** (cards 2674 and 2675). Each person on a decision with no account will be able to have a personal link to it. Linking the person to an account (or to a different account), unlinking them (**None**), changing their address and **Mark left** each empty every such link of theirs, on every decision, in the same save, so a link that was forwarded stops working once the person can sign in, has gone, or is to be reached somewhere else. Delete does the same. Each cancelled link is also recorded on its decision, under your name, with the reason. A new name, job title or groups, and **Mark active**, cancel nothing. The system log's line says how many were cancelled, as `Person 12 "Ann Lee" linked to account 7 "ann"; 2 decision links cleared (by admin)`.

### Add from Accounts

**Add from accounts**, beside **+ Add person**, makes a person of each account you tick: named by the account's username (accounts have no full name, so edit the name afterwards), with the account's address in lower case, and linked to that account.

- **It lists every account no person is linked to, and none is ticked.** Tick each one on purpose; the button says how many will be added ("Add 3 people"). Above the list the dialog warns: "An account's address is set by its holder and is not confirmed: check each is the person you mean before adding them." Adding an account whose holder has set somebody else's address makes that account the directory's entry for the address, and adding the real person later is refused as a duplicate (the `409` then names the account, as above). Delete that entry, or correct its address, before you add the real person.
- **It never links an existing person.** An account whose address is already a person's, in any case, is shown dimmed and cannot be ticked: "matches person Ann Lee: link by hand". As the account's address is not verified, the match is no proof that the account is theirs. If it is, link it by hand from that person's **Edit**, after checking. The server skips such an account whatever is sent.
- **An account linked to a person meanwhile is skipped** too ("already linked").
- **It is all or nothing.** Up to 500 accounts at once; if any id sent is not an account, nothing is added (`400` "userId 12 is not an account").
- **What it did is said afterwards.** "3 people added from accounts" goes after three seconds. Where any account was skipped, the message names each with why and stays until closed: "2 people added from accounts, 1 skipped: sam (address matches person Sam Lee; link by hand)". The system log has one line for the whole step: `2 people added from accounts, 1 skipped (by admin)`.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    Tick["The accounts you ticked"] --> Real{"Every one an account?"}
    Real -->|"no"| None["400: nothing added"]
    Real -->|"yes"| Each["Each account in turn"]
    Each --> Linked{"Already linked to a person?"}
    Linked -->|"yes"| Skip1["Skipped: already linked"]
    Linked -->|"no"| Match{"Its address a person's,<br/>in any case?"}
    Match -->|"yes"| Skip2["Skipped: link by hand<br/>from that person's Edit"]
    Match -->|"no"| Make["A new person: the username,<br/>the address lower-cased,<br/>linked to the account"]
```

### Groups

The groups list shows each group's name, description and how many of its people are active.

- **+ Add group** opens a form: **Name** (1 to 100 characters), **Description** (up to 1,000), and the people to tick, from a list you can search by name or address, active people first and those who left marked "left". **Edit** opens the same form for a group. The people ticked replace the group's people.
- **A name is used once, in any case of any letter.** "finance" beside "Finance", and "ÉCOLE" or "école" beside "École", are refused with `409` "Group name already in use: Finance" (or "…: École"). A group may be renamed to its own name in other capitals.
- **Adding a group is two steps**, the group and then its people. If the people are refused after the group was added, the group is kept and the form stays open on it; **Save** again sends only the people.
- **Delete** asks first and cannot be undone. Only the group and its memberships go; its people stay in the directory. Decision records keep the group names they recorded.

A rename, of a person or of a group, puts the old and the new name in its log line's details as `renamed: { from, to }` (card 2681), so `/audit` shows what a person or a group was called before.

### Through the API

An administrator's API key can read the directory and keep part of it in step (card 2681, API `1.5.0`): an agent or a script that syncs job titles and groups from elsewhere needs no browser. A key of anyone who is not an administrator is refused `403` `Admin access required`. The six operations are in the [API Reference](api-reference.md#people-and-groups-admin); the wrapper's actions are `people`, `people-groups`, `update-person`, `create-people-group`, `update-people-group` and `set-people-group-members`.

| A key can | A key cannot (the People page only) |
|---|---|
| List every person and every group | See anyone's email address or account id |
| Change a person's job title and groups | Add a person, delete one, or Add from accounts |
| Make a group, change its description, set who is in it | Rename a person or a group, or delete a group |
| | Change an address or an account, or Mark left and Mark active |

**Why a key cannot do the rest.** A key cannot give a password, and the People page asks for yours whenever an address or an account is set or ended, because they decide where a decision's invitations and personal links are mailed. A stolen key that could change an address would have the person's next personal link sent to the thief. Marking someone left ends every link they hold, and a name is how an author ticks a person or a group on a decision. So the server refuses those fields `403` from a key, whatever they hold, and refuses by the key itself as well as by the route, so a later route cannot open them by mistake. The answers never carry an address or an account id, and mark every person and group `untrusted: true`: names and job titles are text others wrote.

**Putting a change back.** A key's change writes no activity entry and sends no webhook. Its `PEOPLE` line on `/audit` ends `(by <username>, through the API)` and its details hold `source: "api"`, `credential: "api key"` and `before`: the job title and groups it replaced, the description, or the whole member set it replaced. The system log is pruned by age and count, so every change, by a key or on the People page, is also kept for good in the `people_changes` table (migration 0064, card 2681's code review): who made it, `ui` or `api`, the person or group, and what the changed fields held before and after, never an address or an account id. A deleted person or group keeps its rows there, the group's member set and the person's groups among them, so either can be made again. To undo a change, read its `before` (from the `/audit` line while it lasts, or from `people_changes` in the database or a backup) and set those values again on the People page (or through the API). The wrapper also asks a person to type `yes` before it empties a group, takes anyone out of one, or takes a person out of any of their groups, and refuses each through a request file.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    Key["Administrator's API key"] -->|"read, no addresses"| Dir[("People directory")]
    Key -->|"job title, groups,<br/>group create, description, members"| Dir
    Key -.->|"403: name, address, account,<br/>status, group name, group delete; 405: add"| Page["People page<br/>(asks for your password)"]
    Page --> Dir
    Dir --> Rec[("people_changes:<br/>before and after, kept for good")]
    Dir --> Log["/audit: PEOPLE line,<br/>through the API, before (pruned)"]
```

### A Board's Product Owner

Each board can have a Product Owner (card 2673): the account that decides its decisions by default, once there are decisions. It is set in the board's **Share Board** window, on the **Product Owner** line under the public switch (card 2674).

- **Only the board's owner or an administrator can change it.** They see a list: **None**, then every account that can edit the board, by username and emoji, never an address: the board's creator, people shared on it as Editor or Owner, members of teams it is shared with as Editor (unless a share of their own as Viewer decides for them), and every administrator. A viewer, and someone who can open the board only because it is public, is not listed. Choose one and press **Save**, which does nothing until the choice differs from the one stored.
- **Everyone else who opens Share Board** sees "Product Owner: *name*", or "Product Owner: None".
- **A refusal is said** on the window's error line, in the server's words, and the list goes back to the one stored: `403` "Only the board's owner or an administrator can change its Product Owner", or `400` "productOwnerUserId 7 cannot edit this board: a board's Product Owner must be an account that can edit it" for someone who lost edit access while the window was open.
- **A Product Owner who has since lost edit access keeps it** until someone changes it. The list then shows them as "*name* (cannot edit this board)".
- A choice not yet saved survives the window reading its list again after another change, unless the stored Product Owner changed meanwhile or the account chosen is no longer listed.

Through the API, it is `productOwnerUserId` on `PUT /api/v1/boards/{boardId}`: see the [API Reference](api-reference.md).

### Decisions

**Where drafts are made** (card 2676). On a board, **More → Go to → Decisions** opens the board's decisions (`/board/<id>/decisions`): every decision that is not a draft, to anyone who can open the board, and each draft to its author, its decider, the board's owners and administrators. Anyone who can edit the board has **New decision**, which asks for a title and opens the new draft's editor (`/decisions/<id>/edit`). Its decider starts as the board's Product Owner, while they can edit the board, and as its author otherwise.
