Every card that is not upstream (not in an Options column and not on an ideas board) and has no commitment recorded is committed at the moment it was created and by whoever created it; the "Filled in" line then ends `committed_at and committed_by on <n> card(s) not upstream`. Every upstream card that holds a commitment is cleared of it, and every card that is not archived is cleared of a discard mark: what is cleared is not something filled in, so it is said in a sentence of its own after the "Filled in" line, `Cleared: the commitment of <n> upstream card(s), the discard mark of <n> card(s) that are not archived.`, each part only when there were any; with none, the line reads as it did. **A backup made before 0060** restores with every column an ordinary one, every board a delivery board, nothing discarded, and every card committed from its creation, so it reads as the same boards with no figure changed; the next item then gives each delivery board its Options column. **A backup made after 0060** restores its Options columns, board kinds and marks as they were; imported into an older image, which has the columns only if it was once updated past 0060, they are kept but not kept up to date (see [Going back to the previous image](deployment.md#upstream-kanban-part-one-the-options-column-and-its-conversion)). The same three repairs run at every start, where they find nothing unless an older image was used meanwhile.
- It gives every delivery board its one Options column (card 2527), as every start does: a board with none gets an empty one titled Options at its far left, one with another title is renamed Options, one that is not leftmost is moved there, and a second one becomes an ordinary column, the leftmost keeping the role. The "Filled in" line then ends with `an Options column at the far left of <n> delivery board(s)`, `the title Options on <n> Options column(s)` or `the far-left place on its board for <n> Options column(s)`, and the "Cleared:" sentence after it with `the Options role of <n> second Options column(s), each board keeping its leftmost`, each only when there were any.
- A value the backup leaves empty, in a column where the running database requires one, is given that column's default. A column's WIP limit that was never set becomes 0, which means no limit. The server log says how many rows that was.
- A row that cannot be copied is left out, and the restore is still committed, unless it leaves user accounts and no superadmin among them. The message on screen says how many problems there were and lists them, the first hundred where there are more. The list also carries anything else worth knowing, such as a card in the backup whose column is not in the backup either; that card is copied as it is.
- It will not leave rows pointing, through a foreign key, at a row it left out. If a row that could not be copied is one that other rows refer to in that way, the whole restore is refused and nothing has been changed. References that have no foreign key are not covered by that check: a card's milestone, the two ends of a dependency, a task request's target and a report schedule's scope. A board's grouping is (card 2293): a restore that left out a grouping row the backup held, while a board points at that grouping, is refused in the same way, so that the repair above never takes a board out of a grouping the restore failed to copy.
- The copy runs in one transaction. If it fails, nothing has been changed.
- No session, no unused invite, no request link and no form key is brought in, from a backup of any age. A used invite comes back only from a backup this server sealed, as history, written used whatever the file holds; a backup whose seal does not check out brings in no invite at all (card 3009's code review, FP1). Everybody signs in again, and every request's links are ended: its requester asks for a new one, or gets one in the next mail.
- It puts attachment files back from the trash. A backup holds the database and not the files. When a card is deleted its files are kept in a trash for 30 days, so after an import of a backup taken before the delete, each restored attachment whose file is in the trash has it moved back. The result adds a line, for example "3 attachment files put back from the trash.", when there were any. A file that could not be put back is in the list of problems.
- It does not bring back a file that is not on this server. An attachment restored from a backup of another installation, or deleted more than 30 days ago, shows on its card and cannot be opened unless the file is copied into the uploads folder. See [Attachment Files](deployment.md#attachment-files).

#### Mail Held in the Requests Inbox (card 3020)

A restore of a backup whose seal did not verify holds the mail of each task request whose requester or card this server did not hold as it is (above): the requester's address is only the file's word, and so is the card whose title and report the mail would carry. While a request is held, nothing goes to its requester: no progress mail on its card, no completion report, no accept or decline, no reply and no new link, and asking for a link with its address is answered as for any address, with nothing sent. Progress mail on a card goes to every accepted request on it that is not held, each with a link of its own, so a held request planted on a card hides nobody's mail.

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> Mailed: made here, or restored as this server held it
    [*] --> Held: restored from an unsealed backup, not as this server held it, with an address
    Held --> Held: comment, move, complete, reply, accept, ask for a link: nothing sent
    Held --> Mailed: Release, by an administrator, with password and code, the address as reviewed
    Held --> [*]: unconfirmed: lapses 7 days after it was made
    Mailed --> Held: a later unsealed restore, where it is not as this server held it
```

- **Mail held** in the Requests inbox (`/inbox`) lists, for administrators only, every held request, whoever it was addressed to: its title, requester, address, card and the card's board. Every address is shown with the code points of any character that is not plain ASCII, and every title, name and board in printable form, so a look-alike or a hidden character shows as what it is. A held request also carries a **Mail held** badge in the list.
- **Release** opens a dialog: "Mail to *address* is held: this request came in with a backup whose seal did not verify. Its card: *title* on *board*. Release it only if you recognise the address and the card. Its requester is then mailed progress, replies and the completion report." It asks for your password and a code. It is tied to the address and the card you reviewed: where the request's address, its card, the card's title or the card's board has changed since the list was read, it is refused with `409` "That request's address or card has changed since the list was read: read it again and review it as it is now. Nothing was changed." Releasing mails nothing at once; the next mail carries a link. Who released it, and when, is kept on the request, and one `INFO` line names the request and the administrator, never the address.
- **An unconfirmed request cannot be released**: the list shows "Cannot be released: lapses after 7 days", and a release is refused with `409` "An unconfirmed request cannot be released: it lapses 7 days after it was made. Nothing was changed." Releasing it would send a confirmation link to the file's address.
- **A held request with no address cannot be released** (its requester's account has gone, or it came with none): the list shows "Cannot be released: no address" and offers no Release, and a release is refused with `409` "A request with no address cannot be released: it stays held. Nothing was changed." It stays held.
- **A release is kept only while the request is as this server holds it.** A later restore of a backup whose seal does not verify, in which the request is not as this server held it, holds it again and empties its release record: who released it and when then survives only in the `INFO` line written at the release. A release by an account the restore does not bring back as the same account keeps its date and name, with no account.
- **A reply to a held request** is saved, and the chat says "Mail held – the requester was not mailed".
- The inbox shows every requester's address with its code points and every requester's name in printable form, in the list and in the chat header, held or not.

#### When an Import Is Not Accepted

The page says which of four things went wrong, or that the file is not a backup it takes. It used to say "Invalid backup file" for all of them.

| The page says | What happened | What to do |
|---------------|---------------|------------|
| The file "…" is not a .db backup. Exports in the old JSON format, which DumpFire wrote only from 1 to 7 April 2026, are no longer imported. Nothing was sent | The file's name does not end in `.db`, `.sqlite` or `.sqlite3`, in any case. The page sent nothing | Pick the `.db` file a backup or an export made. A request sent straight to `POST /api/admin/import` whose media type is not `application/octet-stream` or `application/x-sqlite3` (parameters and case aside) is refused `400` before any of it is read, with "Import takes a .db backup, sent as application/octet-stream. Exports in the old JSON format, which DumpFire wrote only from 1 to 7 April 2026, are no longer imported. Nothing was changed."; one sent as either whose bytes are not a SQLite file is refused `400` "Invalid file — not a SQLite database" |
| The backup "…" could not be read on this computer, so nothing was sent | The browser could not read the file | Check the file is still where it was, and pick it again |
| No answer from the server. It is not known whether the backup "…" was accepted | The request was sent and nothing came back | Reload the page and look at what the database holds before trying again |
| The backup "…" was refused before it reached DumpFire, probably a size limit at the proxy in front of it (HTTP 413) | Something in front of DumpFire answered, and not DumpFire. nginx refuses any upload over 1 MB unless it is told otherwise | Raise the proxy's limit for the import: see [Reverse Proxy](deployment.md#reverse-proxy--nginx) |
| The backup "…" is larger than this server accepts. The limit here is 512 MB | DumpFire refused it for its size, and says its limit | Raise `BODY_SIZE_LIMIT`: see [Upload Size Limits](deployment.md#upload-size-limits) |
| The import could not write its working copy beside the database (…). Nothing was changed. Or: Restore failed: The import could not write the safety copy of this server's data beside the database (…). Nothing was changed. | DumpFire could not write one of the two files an import writes beside the database before it replaces anything (card 2925). The brackets say why: no space left on the disk, the server not allowed to write there, a read-only disk, its folder missing (a database held in memory, as the tests use), something else in the way (a folder with the file's name), the file in use, or the error's code. Anything written part-way was removed, and an earlier import's safety copy is left as it was: the new one is written under a temporary name and only then put in its place | Free space on the volume, or check who owns the database's folder and what is in it. The `CRITICAL` "Import failed" or "Database restore failed" line in the system log names the path. See [How Large an Import Can Be](deployment.md#how-large-an-import-can-be) |
| The import could not write its working copy beside the database. Nothing was changed. | The folder the database is in is not there (card 2925). The import never makes it: a server whose database folder has gone is already in trouble | Stop and look at the server before anything else: is the volume mounted where `DB_PATH` says? The `CRITICAL` line names the path |

Anything else DumpFire refuses is shown in its own words. Nothing is changed by any of these but, possibly, the "No answer" one.

**By hand**, when the server cannot be used:

1. Download the `.db` file from your backup destination
2. Stop the DumpFire container
3. Replace `/app/data/dumpfire.db` with the backup file, and delete `dumpfire.db-wal` and `dumpfire.db-shm` from the same directory if they are there
4. Start the container
5. Enter what a backup does not hold (card 2169): the mail password in **Admin → Email / SMTP**, each destination's credentials with **Edit** in **Admin → Scheduled Backups** (a Google Drive destination connected with OAuth: its client secret, then **Connect**; an SFTP destination keeps the host key the file holds, since nothing takes it out of a file put back by hand, so compare the Host key in **Edit** with the server's, `ssh-keygen -lf /etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key.pub`, before typing its password, card 2250), and each signed webhook's secret, switching it on, with `PUT /api/v1/webhooks/{id}`. The server's log says what is missing: one `[WARN] [STARTUP]` line at every start, "To enter again, because a backup does not hold them: … A database put in place by hand from a backup has none of them: see docs/admin-guide.md, Restoring from Backup.", until each is entered, or, for a webhook no longer wanted, deleted

The database runs in SQLite's WAL mode, which keeps recent changes in the `-wal` file beside the database. Those two files belong to the database that was there before. Left beside the backup, SQLite would try to apply the old database's changes to it.

Replacing the file brings back the backup's structure and its migration record along with its data. When the container starts, the migration runner applies whatever migrations the backup has not had.

Nothing checks a file put back by hand. A backup made by this version holds no session, so everybody signs in again; one made before it brings its sessions back with it. A backup made since card 2169 comes up with no mail password, no destination credentials and its signed webhooks off, and nothing can fill them from the database that was there before: that is why step 5 is there, and why a server that still runs is restored with Import Backup instead. The same is true of an Import Backup into an image from before card 2169, after going back to one. A file put back by hand also brings its second factors and recovery codes exactly as it holds them, a factor removed since the backup included, with none of Import Backup's rules (card 2768); and if its factors were made with another key than the one `MFA_KEY_FILE` names, the server refuses to start until it has that key (see [The Key That Protects Authenticator Secrets](deployment.md#the-key-that-protects-authenticator-secrets)). A file made before the key was changed is started once with its old key named as `MFA_KEY_FILE_PREVIOUS`, which moves its factors to the current key (card 2773, see [Restoring After a Change of Key](deployment.md#restoring-after-a-change-of-key)). Import Backup of such a backup needs no key named: accounts still on this server keep their factors, and any other account in it is named as having none, to be reset. And if its users have no superadmin among them, the server starts, says so in the log at `CRITICAL`, and has nobody who can use what is the superadmin's: see [If Nobody Can Sign In as the Superadmin](deployment.md#if-nobody-can-sign-in-as-the-superadmin).

> ⚠️ **After an update, if you have gone back to an older image, do not use Import Backup while the older image is running.** An older image can overwrite the migration record with the backup's, and the newer image will then refuse to start. See [Going Back to the Previous Image](deployment.md#going-back-to-the-previous-image).

---

## Archive Management

### Archived Cards Page

Access from the dashboard **More ⋯** menu → **Archived**.

Features:
- **Search** — find archived cards by title
- **Filter by board** — narrow down to a specific board
- **Restore** — bring the card back to the column it was archived from. A restore that is refused, for a card deleted for good in another tab for instance, says why and leaves the card in the list. A restore writes `card_restored` in the board's activity log, as archiving writes `card_archived`; it wrote nothing until 2026-10-14
- **Permanent delete** — remove the card for good, with what is listed below
- **Purge All** (an administrator's) — every archived card on every board, for good; it asks for your password (card 2701) and a code (card 2770)

### Archiving and Deleting

Archiving a card and deleting it are different things.

| | Archive | Permanent delete |
|---|---------|------------------|
| The card | Hidden from the board, listed on the Archived page | Gone |
| Its subtasks and comments | Kept | Deleted |
| Its attachments | Kept, and still open after a restore | Rows deleted. The files go to a trash for 30 days |
| Its dependency links | Kept. After a restore the card waits on what it waited on | Deleted. Cards that were waiting on it no longer are |
| Its sub-boards | Kept, with everything on them | Deleted, at every depth, with everything on them |
| Can it be undone? | Yes: **Restore** | Only by importing a backup taken before the delete |

Every permanent delete asks for confirmation, and the dialog says what will go. Each is all or nothing: if a delete fails, nothing was deleted and the page says so.

| What is deleted | Where | What goes with it |
|-----------------|-------|-------------------|
| A card | The archive panel of its board, or the Archived page. On the board itself a card can only be archived: its menu, the card's own window and the bulk bar each say **Archive**. The card's window asks first, on a board, in All Tasks and in All To Do alike, and says afterwards where to restore it from; a plan page's card window has no **Archive** | As the table above |
| A column | The column, on its board | **Every card in it, deleted for good and not archived**, each as the table above. Move the cards first if they are to be kept. The board's Options column is never deleted, empty or not (card 2527): its menu offers no Delete column, and the API refuses it |
| A board or sub-board | The dashboard, the board page for a sub-board, or **Admin → Board Management** | Its columns and cards, every sub-board under it, and any report schedules for it. Cards on other boards that were in one of its milestones stay, in no milestone. Its card categories go with it, and its sub-boards': a card on another board that carried one has none. Until 2026-10-14 they stayed, as categories of no board, which every user was offered |
| Every card on a board | **Admin → Board Management → Clear Cards** | Every card, archived ones included, each as the table above. The board, its columns and its categories are kept, except on a board in a loop, which goes too: see [A board beneath one of its own cards](#a-board-beneath-one-of-its-own-cards) |
| Every archived card | **Admin → Danger Zone → Purge Archived Cards**, or the Archived page | Every archived card on every board, each as the table above |

**A board that is no longer there**, deleted in another tab say, is answered "Board not found" by **Clear Cards** and **Clear Categories**, and by a delete of the board from Board Management or the dashboard, and nothing is changed or recorded (card 2211). Each used to say it was done: Clear Cards said "All cards cleared from board", and an administrator's delete "Board deleted", for a board that was gone. Anyone but an administrator is still refused Clear Cards and Clear Categories first, whether the board is there or not.

A column delete, a purge and a clear each leave one entry in the activity log of the board they happened on, saying how many cards went. A board delete leaves none there, because the board's log goes with the board; the system log has an `INFO` line naming the board, who deleted it and what went with it. A card, a column, a purge or a clear whose board went with it, as a board in a loop does, leaves the same kind of line instead of the entry: see [A board beneath one of its own cards](#a-board-beneath-one-of-its-own-cards). Recent system log lines are on the **Audit** page under **Live Console**, and the stored history is at `GET /api/logs`.

Pages open on what a delete takes are told once it is done, and read their board again (card 2108). A page open on a deleted board, or on a sub-board deleted with a board, a card or a column, shows "Board not found". A page open on a board that Clear Cards emptied, or that Purge Archived Cards took cards from, shows the board as it now is, or "Board not found" if the board went with its cards, as a board in a loop does. The page of the board a deleted sub-board hung from is told as well, so its card's sub-board link goes. Until 2026-10-14 each stayed as it was until it was reloaded. What is sent names nothing: no board, card or person. A member's dashboard is not told when a board they can see is deleted, because nobody but an administrator can be told anything of a board that is gone; it catches up the next time it is brought to the front.

## The Options Column

Every delivery board, sub-boards included, keeps ideas apart from committed work in one **Options** column (milestone 22, card 2459; card 2527 made it part of every delivery board): upstream of the commitment point, at the far left of the board, with no WIP limit. A card there is an option, an idea that may or may not be done; moving it out commits it. An ideas board has none.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    New["A new delivery board<br/>or sub-board"] -->|"made with it"| Strip["Options<br/>folded strip: count and aging"]
    Old["A board made before card 2527"] -->|"start-up step, at the first start<br/>of the update and after a restore"| Strip
    Strip -->|"press the strip"| Open["Options column, open"]
    Open -->|"‹ in its header"| Strip
    Open -->|"drag a card out"| Committed["committed work"]
```

- **Every delivery board has one.** A new board or sub-board is made with Options, To Do, On Hold, In Progress and Complete. A board made before the update is given an empty Options column at its far left at the first start of the update, folded, so its own columns do not move; the server log says how many (`Added an Options column at the far left of N delivery board(s) that had none`). A role column that carried another name, from trying a milestone 22 build, is renamed Options, and a second one on a board becomes an ordinary column, its cards committed work. Nobody adds or removes an Options column: More has no Add Options Column, Add Column has no Options tick, and no column's menu makes or stops one.
- **The strip.** The Options column is drawn folded, as a narrow strip with its count, the age of its oldest option, and how many options are older than 30 days. A press opens it into a column; **‹** in its header folds it again, and the keyboard's focus follows to the **‹** or the strip, each saying to a screen reader whether the column is open. Each person's choice is kept in their own browser, per board, so a board opened for the first time, or in another browser, is folded. Cards can be dropped on the strip. On a phone or a window 768 pixels wide or less the board opens scrolled to To Do, the strip a swipe to its left.
- **Rules.** One Options column a delivery board, always called **Options** (no "(Options)" is shown after it), never on an ideas board. It cannot be renamed or deleted: its menu has no Rename and no Delete column, a press on its name does not rename it, and the API refuses both (`The Options column is always called Options.`; `Every delivery board keeps its Options column. Fold it away with the arrow on its strip instead.`). No other column of a delivery board can be called Options, in any case (`Only the board's Options column is called Options. Choose another name.`); a column already called Options before the update keeps its name. No column goes left of it: Add Column offers no "At the start" while it exists, a column dragged left of it lands straight after it, and the API refuses a position at or left of it. It has no WIP limit. Its colour and its sort can be changed as any column's.
- **Archiving or deleting an option discards it** (card 2461). An option archived from the Options column, or from any column of an ideas board, by any way of archiving a card (the card window, the card menu, a bulk archive on the board, or `DELETE` through the API), is discarded by whoever archived it, at that moment: the card records both, its activity entry is **Discarded card** (`"T": discarded from Options`) in place of Archived card, its webhook `card.discarded`, and the Archived page, All Tasks' **Archived Cards** and the board's **Archived Cards** mark it **Discarded** (its tooltip: "Discarded from the Options column. Restoring it makes it an idea again."). The page says so as it happens: the archive's question ends "This idea will be recorded as discarded." and the toast reads `Discarded "T". Restore it from Archived Cards.`, on the board (the card window and the card menu) and in All Tasks' card window; a bulk archive counts the ideas among what it archived (`3 cards archived, 1 of them an idea recorded as discarded.`). All Tasks says so for a card on an ideas board too, whose kind its load carries (card 2463); All To Do lists no upstream column, so has no idea to archive. Restoring it, from any of them or with `archivedAt: null` through the API, clears the discard: the card is back in its column, still an option, and the entry reads **Restored card**, `"T": back in Options`. An option deleted for good writes **Discarded card** (`"T": deleted from Ideas`) and then **Deleted card**, and sends `card.discarded` then `card.deleted`. An archived card deleted for good writes only Deleted card, wherever it is: one discarded already was discarded once, and one archived as committed work before its column was converted to Options, which only a milestone 22 build before card 2527 could do, had left play. Deleting a column or a board, **Clear Cards** and **Purge Archived Cards** discard nothing: they are housekeeping, with no entry for each card.
- **Archiving unfinished work abandons it** (card 2461, ruling f). A committed card archived from a column that is not Complete is recorded as **Archived unfinished card** (`"T": archived unfinished, from In Progress`) in place of Archived card, so work dropped before it was finished is seen; the figures call it "Abandoned" (card 2462, below). Nothing is marked on the card and no webhook is sent, as for any archive; a restore is Restored card as before. A card archived from Complete (or Done) is still **Archived card**; a final column called anything else, such as Released or Shipped, counts as unfinished.
- **Moving across the commitment point** (card 2460). A card moved out of the Options column into another column, by any way of moving a card, is committed by whoever moved it, at that moment: its activity entry is **Committed card**, its webhook `card.committed`, and the page shows `Committed "<title>"` after a drag, an On Hold confirmation, the card menu's **Move to**, a bulk **Move** or All Tasks' **Move** window. Moved back in, its commitment is cleared (**Returned card to Options**, and `Returned "<title>" to Options` on the page). Every **Move to** list names the Options column (one still carrying another name, until the start-up step renames it, is marked "(Options)" after it). Two moves are refused: an archived card across the commitment point, either way (`Card "T" is archived: restore it before moving it out of or back into Options`), and a committed card that has a sub-board back into the Options column (`Card "T" has a sub-board, so it cannot go into the Options column: a card with sub-boards is committed work. Keep it in a committed column, or archive it if it is no longer wanted.`); a card already in the Options column with a sub-board, made before that was refused, can still be moved out. A webhook that asks for every event receives `card.committed` from this release; one that lists only `card.moved` does not, so a receiver that mirrors columns should add it.
- **The figures, reports and what owners see** (card 2462). Options are in none of the delivery figures, anywhere: board **Statistics** (its totals, average age and bars), the board's delivery metrics, the flow diagram, the burndown, the dashboard's board rows and its board health and aging, All Tasks' totals, My Tasks, goals and their plans, reports and the activity report. The exports list them, marked **Option** in their Stage column. For a board whose Options column has never held an option every figure is as it was, but one: a card moved from one Complete column to another after it was completed showed a negative cycle time, and now shows 0.
  - **Statistics** gives the board's options a line of their own, `Options 2 · discard rate 25%`. Its Delivery Metrics name **Lead time (commitment to done)**, from the card's commitment to Complete (for a card made before this release, its creation), and **Cycle time**, the time from start of work to done: from the first move after the commitment, or a commitment straight into a working column (not To Do, On Hold, the board's first committed column or a queue such as Backlog or Ready), never longer than the lead time. A card created straight into a working column has no cycle time. **Throughput** sits beside **Committed (all)**, the cards that arrived as committed work in the period. **Abandoned: N** shows on any board with committed work archived before Complete. Where the Options column holds an option now, or one of the period's figures is not zero, the **Upstream** figures follow (card 2527: every delivery board has an Options column, mostly empty, so the panel no longer shows a block of zeros): Committed from options, Options now, Returned to options, Discarded, Sent to other boards, Discard rate (discarded out of discarded, committed and sent on), Oldest option (days), Options over 30 days, and Time in options (average and median). An ideas board's Statistics show `N ideas`, the Upstream figures and the flow diagram, and none of the delivery tiles, bars, metrics or burndown. When the metrics cannot be read the panel says `Metrics could not be loaded`, with **Retry**; on All Tasks it says `Choose a board to see its delivery metrics` until a board is chosen, and then counts that board alone.
  - **The flow diagram** leaves the Options column out. **Show upstream**, beside its heading on a delivery board whose Options column has something to show (as the Upstream figures above), draws it as the bottom band with the **commitment point** along its top; each person's choice is kept in their own browser, and no band is drawn where the tick is not offered. A column made the Options column during the 30 days, which only a milestone 22 build before card 2527 could do, is left out with its band, its earlier days as a committed column too, until Show upstream is ticked.
  - **The dashboard** gives a board with options a count of its committed cards, whose tooltip says the options are not counted; a board that still holds options is not hidden as done, and sorting by Cards orders an ideas board by its ideas. A goal whose committed cards are all done stays **Ready** with options left, and its tooltip says how many it still holds. **All Tasks** puts every board's options in one **Options** column, first, marked **not committed**, shown only while it holds cards. A goal's list reads `3/5 and 2 options`; its plan notes `and 2 options` under its cards complete, marks each option **Option** in the work table (with an **Options** filter), draws it dashed in the graph and tags it on the critical path. An option is never startable or waiting, but holds up what depends on it.
  - **Reports.** The PDF gives an **Upstream** line when anything happened upstream in the period (`Upstream: 3 options now, oldest 40 days, 1 over 30 days · 2 committed from options · …`), and `Abandoned (committed work archived before Complete): N` on a line of its own when there is abandoned work, which an ordinary board can have too. A report about one person has neither and, where its boards have an Options column, an ideas board or abandoned work in the period, says why: those figures describe the boards, not one person's work. The Reports page's Person hint says the same, naming the same four things (options, commitments from options, discards and abandoned work).
  - **Exports.** Both CSV exports end with two more columns, **Stage** (Option or Committed: the exports list live cards, options included) and **Committed** (when the card was committed); every other column keeps its place.

## Ideas Boards

An **ideas board** (milestone 22, card 2463) is a board of ideas only: every column is upstream, as the Options column is, and its ideas are sent on to delivery boards. Every board made before is a delivery board, and stays one.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    New["Dashboard → New Board<br/>Kind: Ideas board"] --> IB["Ideas board<br/>New Ideas · Shaping · Ready for Replenishment"]
    Conv["Delivery board → More →<br/>Make this an ideas board…<br/>(owner or administrator)"] --> Ask["Confirmation counting<br/>its live and archived cards"]
    Ask --> IB
    IB -->|"card menu or card window:<br/>Send to board…"| Win["Send window: a delivery board you can edit"]
    Win -->|"To its Options"| Opt["its Options column, still an idea"]
    Win -->|"Commit to its To Do"| Todo["its To Do, committed"]
    IB -->|"More → Make this a delivery board…<br/>(only with no cards)"| DB["Delivery board"]
```

- **Making one.** **New Board** asks for the kind: **Delivery board** (the default, Options, To Do, On Hold, In Progress, Complete) or **Ideas board** (New Ideas, Shaping, Ready for Replenishment). A sub-board, made from a card, is always a delivery board, and no sub-board can be made under an idea or an option: the card's menu offers no **Add Sub-board**, its card window no sub-board to create, link or add, and the dashboard's **New Sub-board** is not offered on an ideas board (a sub-board an option already has still opens and can be deleted). The board's header says **Ideas board**.
- **Its columns.** Each always offers **Add idea**, shows the age of its oldest idea and how many are older than 30 days, and may have a WIP limit (on Ready for Replenishment, a replenishment queue). None may be called To Do, On Hold or Complete, or anything that means one (Todo, Blocked, Parked, Waiting, Done): the name is refused, `An ideas board has no Complete column: "Done" would make one. Choose another name.` A column holding ideas is not deleted: the board says so at once, `This column of an ideas board still holds 2 ideas: move them out, or archive them to discard them, first.` More has no **Burndown** and no **Add Options Column** on an ideas board; its Statistics show `N ideas`, the Upstream figures and the flow diagram.
- **Converting a delivery board.** The board's owner or an administrator uses **More → Make this an ideas board…** on a board that is not a sub-board. The board counts its cards, live and archived, and asks: every card becomes an idea, its commitment cleared, in one step recorded as one entry (**Converted board to an ideas board**), with no mail or webhook; the empty To Do, On Hold and Complete columns are removed, and so is the Options column when it holds no card, live or archived (one holding cards becomes an ordinary column). A board with no cards at all has its columns replaced with the ideas board's three. Before asking, the board says why it cannot when a live card has a sub-board (an archived one, or one on a board you cannot open, is refused by the server after the question, in its own words) (`Card "T" has a sub-board, and a card with sub-boards is never an option: move it to another board, or move or delete its sub-board, first.`), when a To Do or On Hold column holds cards (`"To Do" holds 3 cards, and an ideas board has no To Do column: rename it first.`), or when a Complete column holds cards (`… move them to a delivery board, or delete them, first.`). If a card was added after the question, the server refuses (`The board now holds 5 cards; look again.`) and nothing changes. Through the API it is `POST /api/v1/boards/{boardId}/convert` with `{ "kind": "ideas", "cards": N }`.
- **Going back.** **More → Make this a delivery board…** works only on an ideas board holding no cards, archived ones included; its columns are replaced with Options, To Do, On Hold, In Progress and Complete, recorded as **Updated board**. While it holds any, the board says so (`This ideas board holds 2 cards. Making it a delivery board would commit them without anyone committing them: move them to a delivery board, or delete them, first.`).
- **Send to board.** **Send to board…**, in a card's menu and its card window, is offered for an idea to anyone who can edit the board: any card on an ideas board, or in a delivery board's Options column; never for committed work. The Send window lists every delivery board you can edit, and offers **To its Options** (never for a card with a sub-board; greyed only for a board restored from a backup made before card 2527, until the next start gives it its Options column), and **Commit to its To Do**, into the board's first column called To Do, or its first working column, never On Hold, naming that column when it is not To Do. A choice the board cannot take is shown greyed with why, and never chosen. From a card window with changes not saved, the board asks first (**Send without saving**), as the Send closes the window. The card leaves this board, and the focus goes to the column it left; the toast says `Sent "T" to Ops's Options` or `… To Do`, `Committed "T"` for To Do, and, if it left a goal of this board's, that it did. A refusal is said in the server's words and the window stays open. The idea keeps its age; its labels, category and goal stay behind. This board records **Sent card to another board**; any other move of an idea to another board, the card window's board picker or All Tasks' Move, is recorded the same way.

## When to Use Options

What to tell a team adopting the Options column or an ideas board (milestone 22, ruling w; the feature whole, with its rules and API, is in [Upstream Kanban](upstream-kanban.md)):

- **Park ideas in Options, not in On Hold.** On Hold is committed work that is paused: Statistics, the dashboard, reports and goals count it as committed, open work, aged from its commitment. An idea parked there looks like stale delivery work and makes the lead time longer when it is done. A board that has parked ideas in a column such as Backlog or On Hold moves them into its Options column, each a recorded return to options (the board's bulk Move takes many at once); no column is converted into the Options column, since every delivery board has one (card 2527).
- **Discard an idea that is not needed; do not complete it.** Moving an option straight to Complete is allowed, and records a commitment and a completion in the one move, for an idea that was done there and then. It then counts as delivered work in throughput, lead time, the burndown and reports. An idea that turned out not to be needed is archived instead, which records it as discarded and counts it in the discard rate.
- **Returning work to Options rewrites past burndowns.** The burndown is worked out from each card as it is now, so a committed card moved back into Options leaves the scope of every earlier day too, and a chart already sent to someone will read differently. Return a card only when nobody is committed to it any more; it is counted in **Returned to options**.

The tracking wrapper's commands for all of this (`create-board -Kind ideas`, `convert-board`, `send-card`, `upstream`, `cards -Stage`; card 2527 took out `convert-column` and `-Role`, as no column is made the Options column now) and the request each builds are in `.agent/workflows/dumpfire-api-reference.md`, *Upstream*; they need the server to run API 1.3.0 or later.

---

## Attachments

Deleting a file from a card's **Files** tab asks first, in the app's confirm dialog, "Delete File", naming it (card 2360, 2026-10-21; it went on one click).

### Where the files are

Attachment files are on the server's disk, in the `uploads` folder beside the database, and are in no backup that DumpFire makes. Backing that folder up is described in [Deployment](deployment.md#attachment-files).

### The trash

When an attachment's row is deleted, because the attachment itself, its card, its column or its board was deleted, its file is moved to a trash folder and removed 30 days later. Until then, importing a backup taken before the delete brings the attachment back, file included. There is no screen for the trash: files come back through **Import Backup**.

**Files no attachment names go to the trash too** (card 2107). Every six hours, and first 15 minutes after the server starts, DumpFire looks through the card folders of the uploads folder for files that no attachment on any card names: a file left by a delete whose move to the trash failed, by an upload that was cut off, or by a permanent delete made before deleted cards' files went to the trash. Each one that has not changed for a day is moved to the trash, kept 30 days and then removed, like any other. It never touches a file an attachment names, a file written in the last day, or anything that is not a plain file in a card's own folder, and it does nothing at all if it cannot read the attachments. It runs only where the scheduled backups and reports run, so not on a copy started with `SCHEDULERS=off`.

- **The first run after the update that brings it** moves every file left by permanent deletes made before then. The system log says how many in one `[INFO] [UPLOADS]` line on the Audit page's Live Console.
- **Importing a backup within 30 days puts any of them back** that a restored attachment names, as it does for a deleted card's files.
- **A restore that left the attachments out** says so in its result. Its files go to the trash at the next run, and importing a backup that has the attachments, within 30 days, puts them back.

What the sweep reads, what it never touches and every line it writes are in [Deployment](deployment.md#the-sweep-files-no-attachment-names).

### How large an attachment can be

A file of up to 25 MB can be attached to a card. A file that is not attached is listed under the upload area with the reason, one line a file, and the others of the same upload are still tried:

| The card says | What happened |
|---------------|---------------|
| "…" could not be read on this computer, so nothing was sent | The browser could not read the file: a folder was dropped, or the file has moved |
| No answer from the server. It is not known whether "…" was accepted | Open the card again to see whether it is there |
| "…" was refused before it reached DumpFire, probably a size limit at the proxy in front of it (HTTP 413) | The proxy in front of DumpFire refused it. nginx refuses an upload over 1 MB unless it is told otherwise: see [Reverse Proxy](deployment.md#reverse-proxy--nginx) |
| "…" is larger than this server accepts. The limit here is 26 MB | DumpFire refused it for its size. A limit of 512 KB there means the server was started without `BODY_SIZE_LIMIT`: see [Upload Size Limits](deployment.md#upload-size-limits) |

The card used to say nothing when an upload failed, and could go on saying "Uploading..." until it was closed.

### How many files an account can upload

Since card 2910 every file counts, image or not, whichever way it is sent: through the card window or through the account's API keys. No one is exempt, the superadmin included. The figures are fixed in the code, not settings. A refused file is listed under the upload area with DumpFire's words:

| The card says | Why |
|---------------|-----|
| "An account can upload at most 30 files an hour. Nothing was read or written. Try again in …" | Each account has 30 uploads an hour, **shared with its API keys**. Counted before the file is sent on, so a refused upload costs nothing more |
| "An account can upload at most 100 MB a day, and this upload would pass it. Nothing was written. Try again in …" | Each account has 100 MB a day, every file together, shared with its API keys. A refused upload still counts what was sent |
| "Too many uploads are in progress. Nothing was read or written. Try again when the others have finished." | An account can have 3 uploads going at once, and the server 6 among everyone. The card window sends a drop's files one at a time, so this is met only with several windows or scripts at once. Try again after a few seconds |
| "The upload did not arrive within 120 seconds. Nothing was written." | The file was still arriving two minutes after it began, over a very slow connection: try again from a faster one, or send a smaller file. What arrived still counts against the day |
| "Send one file, as the only part of the form, named "file"" | The upload was not made by the card window: a script sent a form with more than one part |
| "This card already has 20 files other than images" | A card holds at most 20 files that are not images, beside its 20 images. Images can still be attached. A card that already held more keeps them |
| "Uploads are paused: the server has too little free disk space, or cannot read how much it has. Nothing was written. An administrator can see which on the admin page." | The disk the uploads are kept on is nearly full, or its free space could not be read (card 2920). Every upload is refused, for everyone, until there is room: see [Uploads Disk Space](#uploads-disk-space-card-2920) |

The counts are kept in memory: restarting the server empties them. The first time an account is refused in an hour or a day, or for having too many uploads at once, the Audit page's Live Console shows one `[WARN] [UPLOADS]` line naming the account; further refusals in the same window add none. Deleting a file gives none of the day back: it sits in the trash for 30 days.

A file's name is cleaned when it is attached: folders, invisible and control characters and `<>:"|?*` are taken out, and the name is cut to 100 characters, its extension kept exactly as sent. A name with nothing left is shown as `file-<date>-<time>` with its extension: a file named `.env`, for instance, is attached as `file-2026-11-18-143000.env`, by the time it was attached (UTC). Its type is kept only when it is a plain one (`text/plain`, `application/pdf`), else it is recorded as `application/octet-stream`. On the server's disk every file is named by DumpFire alone.

### What an image must be

Since card 2761, a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP dropped onto a card goes through the same checks as one sent through an API key, and the card shows DumpFire's reason when one is refused:

| The card says | Why |
|---------------|-----|
| "The body is not a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP image", or "The body is a JPEG image, not the image/png its Content-Type says" | The file is named and typed as an image but its bytes are not that image: a page renamed `.png`, for instance. Nothing is attached |
| "The PNG image is not valid: …" (or JPEG, GIF, WebP), with the reason | It is cut short or malformed, it is animated (an animated GIF, PNG or WebP), or something is stuck on after the end of a PNG, GIF or WebP picture. What a phone stores after the end of a JPEG photo (a motion photo's video, an HDR gain map) is cut off instead, and the photo attached |
| "The image is … pixels: the most accepted is 8000 on a side and 40,000,000 in all" | Larger than any screenshot or photo needs |
| "This card already has 20 images" | A card holds at most 20 images, however they were attached. Other files can still be attached |
| "This card was closed more than 180 days ago, so its images are moved to the trash: reopen or restore it to attach one" | [Image retention](backup-reporting.md#image-retention) would move it at its next run |

What is kept of an accepted image: the picture as sent, with its hidden data taken out (location, camera make and model, dates, comments, thumbnails). A JPEG keeps only its "which way up" tag, so a phone photo still shows upright. Its name keeps what was typed but takes the extension of what the file really is (`photo.PNG` is shown as `photo.png`). Up to 25 MB, as for any file.

An image counts against the account's hour and day like any file: see [How many files an account can upload](#how-many-files-an-account-can-upload).

Every other file (PDFs, documents, spreadsheets, and an image the browser does not call one of the four types, such as HEIC) is not checked as an image and is only ever saved to disk when opened. Since card 2910 it counts against the account's hour and day, a card holds 20 of them, and its name is cleaned and its type tidied, as described above.

**At the first start after the update that brings card 2761**, DumpFire checks every image already attached, once, in the same way: one with hidden data (or a video or gain map after a JPEG photo) is cleaned in place, and one refused by the check (not the image its type names, animated, too large, or malformed) is changed to a plain download, kept as it was, hidden data included, and never shown in the browser again. The same check runs straight after an Import Backup, since the backup may be from before the update. The Audit page's Live Console shows one `[WARN] [UPLOADS]` line, "Checked the *n* images stored before card 2761: …", with the counts and the attachment ids of any changed to downloads, any that could not be checked and any given up. A start after that says nothing. If the line says some "could not be checked", the next start checks again; a file that fails the same way at three starts is given up and named, so that the check can finish: see [Deployment](deployment.md#what-the-first-start-says-card-2761) for what to do then, and [Authentication and Security](authentication-security.md#the-images-stored-before-card-2761).

### Attachments that download

What an attachment does depends on how it is opened, and in both cases on the type recorded at upload agreeing with the file name's extension:

| Opened | Shown in the browser | Saved to disk |
|--------|----------------------|---------------|
| From its card | PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP images. Each has a thumbnail on the card, and clicking the thumbnail opens the image | Every other attachment. Clicking the name of any attachment, an image included, saves it |
| By its address, pasted into the browser | PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP images and `.txt` text files, each as its own type | Everything else: PDF, HTML and SVG files, Office documents, archives, and any file whose name and recorded type disagree |

An HTML or SVG file can carry a script, and opened as a page of DumpFire it would run with the reader's session. So neither is ever shown in the browser, and every attachment is answered with `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. A PDF is saved too: a browser shows one in its own viewer, which does not start under that sandbox. If someone reports that an attachment does not open, that is why, and the file is intact.

---

## Danger Zone

The **Danger Zone** at the foot of the Admin panel has four actions. Each asks for confirmation, and all but Vacuum for your password too (card 2701). The first three are any administrator's. **Reset Everything** is shown to the superadmin only, and is refused to everybody else: whoever sets DumpFire up after a reset is its superadmin.

| Action | What it does | What it does not do |
|--------|--------------|---------------------|
| **Purge Archived Cards** | Permanently deletes every archived card on every board, with its subtasks, comments, dependency links and sub-boards. Attachment files go to the trash for 30 days | Touch a card that is not archived |
| **Vacuum Database** | Rebuilds the database file to reclaim unused space, and scrubs the old text of the stored secrets from it and its write-ahead log (card 2898). If something else is reading the database, it says the scrub did not finish ("The database was compacted, but its write-ahead log is held by a reader …"): run it again once nothing else is reading it. The server also tries again on its own every 30 minutes | Change any data, or reset ids. New boards and cards are still numbered on from the highest there has been |
| **Reset All XP** | Sets every user's XP to 0 | Change anything else |
| **Reset Everything** | Clears the whole installation. See below | Restart ids, or clear the system log, the backup history or the record of ended API keys |

### Reset Everything

Reset returns DumpFire to the state of a new installation, ready to be set up again.

| Cleared | Kept |
|---------|------|
| Every board, column, card, subtask, comment, label, category and milestone, and the activity log of every board | The system log, including the line that says who ran the reset. `GET /api/logs` returns it |
| Every user, session, API key, team, invite and membership, and the key remembered browsers are signed with | The backup history, and the record of every API key ended here (card 2995): each key the reset clears is added to it first, so no import of an older backup brings one back; and the record of account endings (card 2996), to which every account but the superadmin is added as disabled first, with the fingerprint of its password hash as replaced (card 2997) |
| Every setting: the mail settings, the application URL, the backup schedule and its destinations | The structure of the database and its record of applied migrations |
| Task requests, report schedules, webhooks, XP, chart history | The id counters: new boards and cards are numbered on from the old ones |
| Every attachment row. The files are moved to the trash for 30 days | Backups already made at the backup destinations |

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    Click["Reset Everything, confirmed"] --> Acc["Every account but the superadmin<br/>recorded as disabled (card 2996)<br/>and its password as replaced (card 2997)"]
    Acc --> Rec["Every API key recorded as ended (card 2995)"]
    Rec --> Tx["One transaction clears every table<br/>but the system log, the backup history,<br/>the record of ended API keys, the record of<br/>account endings and the record of access changes"]
    Tx -->|"Any table cannot be cleared"| Fail["Nothing is cleared.<br/>The page shows 'Reset failed' and the reason,<br/>and stays where it is"]
    Tx -->|"Committed"| Files["Attachment files are moved to the trash"]
    Files -->|"All moved"| Setup["You are signed out<br/>and sent to /setup"]
    Files -->|"Some could not be moved"| Notice["The page shows 'Reset done' and lists them.<br/>'Continue to set-up' goes to /setup"]
    Click -->|"No answer from the server"| Unknown["The page shows 'Reset not confirmed'.<br/>Reload to find out"]
```

- **It is all or nothing.** Either every table is cleared or none is. A reset can no longer leave users in place with their sessions and the settings gone.
- **If it fails, the page says so.** A dialog titled **Reset failed** shows "The reset failed and nothing was cleared:" followed by the reason, and stays until it is closed. Nothing has changed and you are still signed in. The failure is also in the system log, at `ERROR`.
- **If it succeeds, you are signed out** and taken to `/setup` to create the first account. The system log has a `CRITICAL` line saying who reset the database, how many rows went from how many tables, how many API keys were recorded as ended ("N API key(s) recorded as ended, so that no import brings one back, and that record kept"), and how many attachment files were moved to the trash.
- **An import after a reset does not bring the keys back** (card 2995), **and brings every account back disabled** (card 2996) **and needing a password** (card 2997). It brings back the accounts as the backup has them, but not one API key the reset cleared: their holders make new ones. Every account but the superadmin comes back disabled by the superadmin, since the reset recorded it so, and with a password nothing matches, since the reset recorded the password each held as replaced: the superadmin enables, on the Users page, the ones that are wanted, and then sets each one's password (🔑) or sends it an invite (📧). The import's result counts them among the accounts that need their password set. The superadmin's own password is not recorded by a reset, so the backup's superadmin comes back with the password the backup holds. The reset's `CRITICAL` line and its answer count them: "N account(s) recorded as disabled, so that an import brings each back disabled, and that record kept".
- **Attachment files that cannot be moved are reported, and the reset still stands.** A dialog titled **Reset done** lists them. They are where they were, in the uploads folder, and belong to no card.
- **The record of access changes is kept, and the reset records nothing in it** (card 3002). An import after a reset is capped by it as any other: a board, team or person link removed before the reset stays removed.
- **Ids carry on.** The first board created after a reset does not get id 1 again. Nothing left over from before, such as a file in the trash or a line in the system log, can be mistaken for something new.
- **Take a backup first.** Reset cannot be undone except by importing one.

---

## Board Categories

Organise boards into categories for the dashboard sidebar:

1. Go to **Admin → Board Categories** (or use the dashboard category manager: **Display → 🏷️ Manage Categories** beside Your Boards, since 26 October 2026, when its button moved into the Display menu)
2. Create categories with names and colours
3. Assign boards to categories

Categories appear as collapsible groups in the dashboard sidebar.

The dashboard's **Manage Categories** window says a change it could not make under its header, in the server's words or as "No answer from the server. The category may not have been created." (saved, deleted), and keeps what was typed (card 2358, 2026-10-21; it said nothing, and a change with no answer left the window's buttons dead). Deleting a category asks first in the app's confirm dialog, "Delete Category", naming it.

From the keyboard (card 2365, 2026-10-21; it took no focus, Tab walked the page behind it, and Escape did nothing): as the window opens the name box under **Create New** has the focus; Tab and Shift+Tab stay in the window; **Escape** closes it, as × does, except while a change is on its way, so that what the server says of it is not lost (× likewise since card 2375, 2026-10-21: it closed the window and the answer was said to nobody; it is marked unavailable to a screen reader meanwhile); with a category's edit open, Escape cancels that edit alone, and the focus goes back to its ✎ (the next Escape closes the window); and as it closes the focus goes back to the **Display** button whose menu it was opened from (card 2466, 2026-10-26; it went back to the **🏷️ Manage Categories** button, which is now an item of that menu). While the delete's confirm is up, Escape closes the confirm alone.

## Notification Preferences

Each user controls their own notification preferences from **My Account**, under **Email Notifications**. The switches, in the order the page shows them:

| Switch | Controls |
|--------|----------|
| **All Email Notifications** | The master switch. Off, no mail of any kind below is sent, whatever its own switch says. Turning it off turns every switch below off, and turning it on turns them all on |
| **Task Assignments** | Mailed when you are assigned to a card |
| **Comments** | Mailed when someone else comments on a card of a board you are a member of, yourself or through a team |
| **Mentions** | Mailed when someone @mentions you in a comment on a card of a board you can open. Someone named on a board they cannot open is not mailed, and the comment's author is not told (card 2198). A mention is `@` and the username as it is spelled, in any case: `@Greg`, `@jane.doe`, `@Jane Doe` (card 2207) |
| **Card Moves** | Mailed when someone else moves a card you are assigned to to another column on a page, or completes it by any route, through the API included. Never for a move you made yourself, nor for a card on a board you cannot open (card 2180). Also decides the "Unblocked" mail |
| **Task Requests** | Mailed when someone sends you or your team a task request, or replies to one |
| **Request Progress** | For a request you raised while signed in: mailed as work goes on on the card made from it (a move, a subtask ticked, a comment), and when it is complete. Someone who raised a request without an account is always mailed |
| **Board Sharing** | Mailed when a board is shared with you |
| **Planning** | Mailed when someone else makes a plan from cards that depend on each other, and you are assigned to one of its cards or are an administrator |
| **Decisions** | "When someone asks about or raises a concern on a decision you decide or wrote, your question about a decision is answered, or a decision you have not answered is about to close." Mailed when a participant raises a concern about the ruling of a decision you decide (card 2679); (card 2678) when a participant asks a question about a decision you decide or wrote and may answer now, never for your own question, and when a question you asked is answered; and (card 2685) once a round, about a day before the closing time, when your view is asked on a decision and you have not yet responded. Each notice names nobody and quotes nothing; the concern and question notices are at most one about the same participant in 24 hours. A decision's record mails (its invitation, ruling and withdrawal) are not under any switch: a participant must be told. A participant with no account has no switch, and is sent the reminder with no link in it |

The page, the route that saves the switches (`/api/account/notifications`) and the mail that reads them share one list, `src/lib/notification-prefs.ts` (card 2146). Until 2026-10-15 they kept three: **Request Progress** was offered and never saved, so it went back on at every reload and the progress mail was always sent, and **Mentions** was neither offered nor saved, so mention mail could be stopped only by switching all mail off. Nobody had either stored, so anyone who switched Request Progress off before then gets the mail until they switch it off again. A switch that is refused, or gets no answer, goes back to what it was, with the reason under the switches; the switches cannot be pressed while a change is being saved.
